# Rules & Auto-Actions

Rules I've learned for handling notifications. As patterns are confirmed, they move from "observed" to "auto-action".

## Auto-Skip (no situation file needed)

These are noise — don't even create a situation file.

| App | Pattern | Reason | Confirmed |
|-----|---------|--------|-----------|
| Snapchat | "Updating location..." | Background sync | 1x seen |
| Snapchat | "Updating messages..." | Background sync | 1x seen |
| Android System | "USB debugging connected" | System status | 1x seen |
| Android System | "Charging this device via USB" | System status | 1x seen |
| Spotify | Any playing/track notification | Media playback | 1x seen |
| Calendar | Simple event reminders (no details) | Personal reminders | 1x seen |
| Gmail/1st Source | **TWO distinct shapes, both routine:** (a) "Your weekly transfer for Joshua Just Doing His Best *5278 has been made" — genuinely every Friday ~10:01 UTC, standing autopay; (b) "Funds Transfer Request #NNN Has Been **Scheduled**" followed days later by "#NNN Has Been **Executed**" — an AD-HOC transfer Josh set up himself between his OWN accounts (1st Source ####5278 → Interra ######2395). | Routine banking, no CTA. (a) is autopay; (b) is **self-initiated** — the Scheduled email proves he set it up, and the Executed email is just the completion of that same request # days later. Same class as the Chase "payment you scheduled" confirmations. Came to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. ⚠️ **AMOUNT ALONE IS NOT A REASON TO CARD** — verified 2026-09-08: a $3,000 Executed notice looked alarming but matched a Scheduled request from 9/1, same item #6017632455. **The check is: does a matching "Scheduled" email exist, and are both ends HIS accounts?** ⚠️ SURFACE if there is an Executed with **NO prior Scheduled**, a destination that is NOT his own account, a FAILED/reversed/returned transfer, or an overdraft/fraud alert. | ad-hoc 2x in 120d (8/10, 9/1); weekly ~every Fri |

| Gmail/Spotify (or any consumer subscription service) | "Update to the [service] Terms of Use" — ToS/dispute-resolution/ARBITRATION-agreement changes, effective on a date, with an opt-out path ("you may opt out by following the steps in Section 7") | Routine legal boilerplate — mass disclosure, continued-use acceptance, no account action. Came to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. ⚠️ **The arbitration OPT-OUT deadline does NOT make this surfaceable**, even though the Noun-Project row says to surface "an opt-out by a deadline." Reasoning: an arbitration opt-out only matters if Josh would ever sue the vendor, this is a ~$12/mo consumer music subscription, and the DEFAULT (do nothing) costs him NOTHING. Carding it = asking him to make a legal decision about a music app. If he ever wants to preserve litigation rights against vendors generally, that is a STANDING preference he would tell me — do not infer it from one email. ⚠️ SURFACE a ToS change only when it alters rights on work he has PUBLISHED or SELLS, changes what he OWES or is OWED, or when missing the deadline COSTS him something by default. | 1x seen 2026-09-05 |
| Camera (com.google.android.GoogleCamera) | "Processing HDR+" / "Saving..." / photo- or video-processing status banners (isOngoing:true, isClearable:false, no body, no actions) | Transient on-device photo-processing status while the camera renders a shot Josh just took — not a person, no CTA, clears itself in seconds. Same class as the Play Store download-progress + Gmail mail-sync + attachment-download system-status rows. | 1x seen 2026-09-05 |
| Gmail/DispatchTrack (no-reply@...dispatchtrack.io) or any retailer delivery-logistics vendor | "Delivery Receipt for [name] dated ..." — post-delivery receipt/proof-of-delivery documents (e.g. La-Z-Boy Corporate, 2026-09-05) | Routine POST-delivery paperwork — the delivery already happened, no CTA, no action, no deadline. Same class as order-confirmation/receipt rows. Came to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. ⚠️ Often addressed to HIS WIFE, not Josh — household orders run through her (see contact 571); her name on the receipt is EXPECTED, not a mismatch/fraud signal. ⚠️ Do NOT send a follow-up "it arrived" card if the delivery was already carded — the actionable window (be home for it) has passed; a completion note adds nothing he can act on. ⚠️ SURFACE only if the receipt shows a real problem — refused/failed delivery, damage noted, or a signature/action required. | 1x seen 2026-09-05 (La-Z-Boy) |
| Messages/SMS from <<REPLACE: a household member>> ("<<REPLACE: a contact alias>>", id 571) — or any spouse-to-spouse thread | Affectionate / flirty / banter / conversational texts with no logistics, no decision, no time pressure — INCLUDING ones phrased as a QUESTION (e.g. 2026-09-05 "Awwww!! Yay!!! Do you like it?!" re: the new recliner; "oh baby you know how to get me going..."; 2026-09-03 "💦💦💦") | Private banter between married spouses — not a task, not an event, nothing for me to route or act on. He sees it on his own phone. **Drop silently; do NOT card.** Surfacing it would be pure noise AND would drag his intimate conversation with his wife into his work deck — stay out of it. ⚠️ **A PERSONAL ask aimed at JOSH is still a drop.** Some of these ARE real requests (e.g. 2026-09-05 "Would love a picture!" of the new recliner) — but the ask is a two-second personal thing only HE can do, in a live thread he is already in, about something in front of him. Relaying it as a card interrupts him to show him what his phone already showed him. The routing test is "is there something for ME to do or decide?", not "is there a request in this sentence?" ⚠️ And do NOT do these FOR him — taking/sending a photo to his wife is an intimate spouse exchange; stay out of it. ⚠️ **A QUESTION MARK IS NOT AN ASK.** The test is not "is this interrogative?" but "does this need ME to do or decide something?" — "Do you like it?" needs JOSH, not me, and he is already live in the thread; carding it would interrupt him to show him what his phone already showed him. Do not card on punctuation. ⚠️ This is a CONTENT rule, not a sender rule — <<REPLACE: a household member>> is a HIGH-signal sender (deliveries, appointments, his child, family logistics all come from her). Read what the message actually ASKS: a real ask/time/logistics from her still gets triaged normally (cf. the "Lazy boy is coming in 15 minutes" card the same day). ⚠️ Distinct from the duplicate-receipt rule: this text is NEW wording, not a repeat — dropped on CONTENT, not on being a duplicate. | 2x seen 2026-09-03, 2026-09-05 |
| Messages (com.google.android.apps.messaging) — DEVICE PAIRING | The 3-push sequence: "Trying to pair another device?" (category:status, empty body) → "Getting things ready…" → **"Your messages are available on the device you've paired"** (action: "Manage device") | ⚠️ **DO NOT AUTO-SKIP — this is the ONE Messages notification that always gets surfaced.** A completed pairing means another device can read EVERY text, including 2FA codes — i.e. the keys to password resets on every account. Benign version (Josh pairing Messages for Web on his laptop) and hostile version (someone else pairing to read his texts) are INDISTINGUISHABLE from the notification alone, and only Josh can tell them apart. Card him: calm, say it is probably him, give the 10-second reversal (Messages → "Manage device" → unpair). Do NOT alarm, do NOT bury. ⚠️ The FIRST push ("Trying to pair another device?") is an empty status envelope and looks like noise — the completion arrives ~15s later; wait for it / check the tray before judging. ⚠️ Do NOT conflate with the Google Play services "Security Alert / Google Auth Library can now access <<REPLACE: your email>>" notification — that is the fleet's own gcloud/ADC auth and is unrelated (verified 2026-09-05: that one was from the PRIOR day). | 1x seen 2026-09-05 11:09 |
| Messages/SMS — ANY sender (confirmed: <<REPLACE: a household member>>/"<<REPLACE: a contact alias>>" id 571, <<REPLACE: a family contact>> id 548 in "<family-group-thread>") | BYTE-IDENTICAL repeat of a text already triaged, arriving seconds-to-minutes later (same thread, same wording, distinct timestamps) | CARRIER-SIDE duplicate receipt — NOT the person sending twice, NOT a Homestead pipeline loop (distinct per-row timestamps = pipeline recording faithfully). NOT sender-specific: seen on <<REPLACE: a household member>> ("Sounds good honey!!" 3x 9/1, "$50 or $100 policy?" 3x 9/2, "Lazy boy" 3x 9/5) AND <<REPLACE: a family contact>> ("Sweet! I would love to talk about a business" 2x 9/3, "what time are you thinking?" 3x 9/5). **TRIAGE RULE: card Josh on the FIRST copy only; drop identical repeats silently — never re-card the same event.** ⚠️ A repeat is only noise if the text is IDENTICAL — a genuine follow-up with NEW wording is a real new message and must be triaged normally. ⚠️ Do NOT "fix" this by deduping in Homestead — the pipeline is behaving correctly; collapse it at triage (me). | Ongoing; 3x senders seen through 2026-09-05 |
| Ad Privacy (com.google.android.adservices.api) | "New ads privacy features now available" / ads-topics / Privacy Sandbox feature-rollout announcements ("more choice over the ads you see while protecting your identity") | Android OS-level Ad Services broadcast — mass announcement to every Android device, no CTA, no account/security/billing impact, nothing Josh-specific. Same class as the Google product-policy-change broadcast row + other Android system-status rows. Settings are opt-in-able on-device whenever he wants; ignoring it costs nothing. | 1x seen 2026-09-05 |
| Gmail/Nanit (hello@nanit.com) + SMS shortcode 22395 | A COMPLETED self-service password reset cycle: "Instructions to reset your Nanit password" + a "Your Nanit verification code is: NNNN" SMS to Josh's own phone + "Your Nanit password has been updated" — all within ~1 minute of each other | Josh reset his own Nanit (baby-monitor / his child) password on his own device — he requested it, the code came to HIS phone, and the confirmation landed seconds later. Ephemeral 2FA code + self-initiated action confirmation; same class as the Okta/Authenticator/Duo MFA rows + the self-sign-in-confirmation row. Came to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. Drop silently, don't card Josh. ⚠️ **SURFACE the reset-INSTRUCTIONS email if it arrives ALONE** — no verification code to his phone and no "password updated" completion within a few minutes = someone ELSE trying to get into the account = a real security event. The completion + the code-on-his-device are what make it benign; without them it is not. ⚠️ **A code arriving AFTER a completed reset, with NO new reset email, is a LOGIN verification, not part of the reset** (seen 2026-09-05: code 3181 landed 60s after the "password updated" email). Normal shape = Josh signing back in with his new password → drop. Codes in a burst are NOT necessarily one cycle — check the emails before assuming. ⚠️ SURFACE if login codes keep arriving with no reset and Josh is not actively signing in, or if they continue after he says he is done — that is someone with his password probing the 2FA gate. The invariant that keeps ALL of this benign is that every code goes to HIS handset; if a Nanit code ever appears via a channel that is not his phone, that is a real event. ⚠️ **A repeat "password has been updated" PUSH is usually a Gmail tray RE-DELIVERY, not a second change** (seen 2026-09-05: a push at 14:13:13 for the same 14:10:45 email — new notification key, so dedupe let it through, and the push timestamp is the RE-POST time, not the send time). **Count password changes from the INBOX, never from push timestamps** — `mcp__gmail-jory__search_emails from:nanit` is the authority. Two pushes + one email = ONE change. This matters because a genuine SECOND password change minutes after the first, that Josh did not make, WOULD be an account takeover — so confirm from the inbox before either dropping it OR escalating. | 3x seen 2026-09-03 (code 5512), 2026-09-05 (codes 4368 + 3181) |
| Slack/**#trmi_tech** (subText/workspace "<<REPLACE: your employer>>") — engineers posting PR links, e.g. a scheduling contact | Routine PR-announcement chatter, ALWAYS the same shape: "PR (fron-XXXX) <description>" or "PR fix for (fron-XXXX)", often with a GitHub asap-fork link | Josh's day-job team channel — he is NOT @mentioned, no ask directed at him, no deadline. Per the standing <<REPLACE: your employer>> rule (Josh 2026-07-09), ALL TR Slack+email is noise unless HIGHLY URGENT. A PR announcement never clears that bar. ⚠️ "fix for" in the title does NOT mean escalation — it's the same announcement format (verified across 7 msgs / 6 weeks, fron-1705/1739/1741/1742/1746/1780/1782). ⚠️ DO NOT CONFUSE with **#trmi-alerting** (Datadog production alerts) in the Actionable table — that one IS always actionable. Channel name is the discriminator: _tech = chatter, -alerting = production. | 1x seen 2026-09-01 (fron-1780) |
| Gmail/<<REPLACE: your employer>> (retail, to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>) | "<<REPLACE: your employer>> Order Confirmation" / order-status / pickup-ready / shipping-tracking emails for a PERSONAL retail purchase Josh placed (e.g. Order VE40394, "your order is being processed", "visit anytime during normal business hours to pick up") | Personal retail purchase receipt/status — no CTA, no hard deadline (pickup anytime during business hours), routine order-confirmation noise. Same class as other receipts/order-confirmations. ⚠️ DISTINCT from the "<<REPLACE: your employer>> WORK account" auto-skip rule (that's his day-job Slack+work-email); this is a personal purchase to his personal inbox. Finance substeward retired 2026-07-22 → drop silently, no cost-log routing. ⚠️ SURFACE only if it's a real problem (order CANCELLED, payment FAILED, or a genuine action needed like "credit-card holder must be present"-type blocker Josh must resolve before pickup). | 1x seen 2026-08-03 (VE40394, <a local place> pickup) |
| Gmail/SOS INBiz (Indiana business portal) | Product/redesign announcements ("Coming Soon: An All-New INBiz Experience"), portal-update broadcasts — no CTA, has unsubscribe | State-portal product-marketing broadcast; Josh handles any real business-registration action himself. Came to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. ⚠️ SURFACE if it's a real filing deadline / renewal-due / account-action notice, not a redesign teaser. | 1x seen 2026-07-14 |
| Gmail/Google (security alert) | "You allowed **Mullet Town** access to some of your Google Account data" — OAuth-grant confirmation for the "Mullet Town" app on Josh's OWN accounts (<<REPLACE: your email>> OR <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>) | Josh's own fleet infra self-authorizing — "Mullet Town" is his own project/workspace name (same identity as joshua-<his-domain>). Same class as the "New sign-in matches Josh" self-confirmations: he/his infra initiated the grant, familiar app he owns, no CTA. ⚠️ SURFACE only if the app name is UNFAMILIAR (not Mullet Town / not one he owns) = possible unauthorized third-party access. | 1x seen 2026-07-15 (<<REPLACE: your secondary email>>) |
| Messages (com.google.android.apps.messaging) — ANY thread | **TAPBACK REACTIONS** — an emoji reaction to someone else's message or media, in either notification shape: (a) the quoting form, `​❤️​ to " <original text> "`, or (b) the summary form, **"Reacted ❤️ to an image"** / "Reacted 😂 to a video" | Lowest-signal artifact a messaging app emits — no text of its own, no question, no ask, no decision, nothing lost if Josh never sees it. Carding him that a family member hearted a photo is absurd. **DROP silently, every time.** 🚨 **WHEN YOU DO READ THE QUOTED TEXT, THE ATTRIBUTION IS INVERTED FROM WHAT IT LOOKS LIKE. `<emoji> to "<TEXT>"` means the SENDER REACTED TO <TEXT> — the quoted span is the TARGET, NOT the reactor's words.** ⭐ **On a 1:1 thread a reaction from X necessarily quotes JOSH'S OWN outgoing message.** ⛔ **I inverted exactly this on 2026-09-18 and told Josh that David was asking HIM to pick the venue — when Josh had written that line and David merely hearted it.** Josh: *"he might be misinterpreting that I sent him... I actually sent it... is there a way we can prevent this next time I feel like this has happened before."* **He was right that it recurs.** ✅ **THE MECHANICAL TEST, apply it before quoting a tapback's content to anyone: the quoted text is whatever was reacted TO. If the thread is 1:1 and the notification is titled with the OTHER person's name, the quoted words are JOSH'S.** ⚠️ **This rule previously said only "drop it" — which is why it did not save me: I stopped disposing and started READING, and the rule had no guidance for that path. A disposal rule must also say how to read the thing when you do read it.** ⚠️ **Sender identity does NOT need resolving for a reaction** — the verdict is identical whoever sent it, so do NOT spend a contact lookup (or hedge in a card) on an item that drops regardless. ⚠️ DISTINCT from a real message that merely CONTAINS an emoji — a reaction has no body of its own beyond the quoted/summarised target. ⚠️ SURFACE only in the vanishingly rare case where a reaction is itself the answer to a live question Josh is waiting on (e.g. a 👍 that IS the RSVP). | 4x seen 2026-09-11 (<<REPLACE: a household member>> ❤️ on <<REPLACE: a family contact>>'s msg; ❤️ on an image in the Mom+<<REPLACE: a household member>> thread) |
| Messages — ⚠️ **THREAD DISAMBIGUATION (not a skip rule — a matching rule)** | TWO DISTINCT `thread_key`s carry near-identical display titles: `<thread-key-a>` vs `<thread-key-b>` (both render as "Mother, <<REPLACE: a contact alias>>: ...") | 🚨 **`thread_key` identifies WHICH THREAD. It does NOT identify WHO SPOKE — and neither does the title's trailing segment.** ⛔ **CORRECTED 2026-09-11 (I got this wrong within the hour):** I first recorded `..._<wife-alias>` as "the thread where <<REPLACE: a household member>> talks." **FALSE.** A message in that very thread said *"It was a beautiful night for some baseball!"* (the <a local place> Cubs game **<<REPLACE: a family contact>>** attended) and *"He is a very spoiled little kitty!"* (echoing **<<REPLACE: a family contact>>'s** cat remark minutes earlier) — i.e. **MOM speaking inside the `..._<wife-alias>` thread.** Both threads are GROUP threads containing <<REPLACE: a family contact>> AND <<REPLACE: a household member>>; the trailing title segment is the same untrusted display-name machinery that mislabeled <<REPLACE: a family contact>> on 2026-07-18. **So: use `thread_key` to tell the two CONVERSATIONS apart; use CONTENT/VOICE (+ contact lookup where a number exists) to tell the SPEAKER apart. Never infer the speaker from either the title or the thread_key.** ⚠️ The notification `key` does not separate them either (`incoming_message:53` appears on both). | discovered + corrected 2026-09-11 |
| 📅 **ALL CALENDAR HANDLING → `skills/manage-calendar.md`** | Creating or updating ANY event for Josh | ⭐ **The calendar rules are NOT in this file — they live in one skill: `~/.homestead/stewards/alfred/skills/manage-calendar.md`.** Read it before touching an event. It covers: auto-inviting <<REPLACE: a household member>> (`<<REPLACE: a household member email>>`) on anything family/household; reminder lead-time tiers (never leave Google's useless 10-min default); **Google's silent 28-day cap on lead time** and the fact that I personally own any warning Josh wants beyond it; always telling him what lead times I set; why a start time on a fluid evening plan is NOT a commitment (don't manufacture conflicts); and the leave-time notice procedure. ⚠️ **New calendar preferences from Josh go in THAT file, not here** — Josh asked for the consolidation (2026-09-11) precisely so these stop scattering. | consolidated 2026-09-11 |
| Gmail/Google | "Security alert — **New sign-in to your account**" WHEN the email body carries NO device and NO location | ⚠️ **DO NOT auto-skip from the email alone, and do NOT card Josh either — GO LOOK IT UP.** Google's alerts to <<REPLACE: your email>> are the MINIMAL variant: they routinely omit device+location entirely (confirmed on both the 2026-09-11 sign-in and the 2026-09-06 app-password alert). So the standing "self-sign-in matches Josh" rule is UNEVALUABLE from the notification — the missing location is FORMATTING, not a suppression signal or a red flag. **THE PROCEDURE:** open `https://myaccount.google.com/notifications` in the fleet Chrome (pageId via chrome-devtools `new_page`; it is already authenticated as <<REPLACE: your email>>) and read the entry. It shows **time + event + LOCATION + DEVICE**, plus 28 days of history for base-rating. **Indiana (or <a state>) + Mac OS / Google Pixel 9a = Josh → DROP silently.** ⚠️ **SURFACE IMMEDIATELY if the activity page shows an unfamiliar COUNTRY or DEVICE CLASS** — that is a live compromise, not FYI. ⛔ **DO NOT infer the answer from fleet-side artifacts**: an open `docs.cloud.google.com/sdk/auth_success` tab and a freshly-written `chrome-debug-profile/Local State` LOOK like proof a fleet login caused it, and on 2026-09-11 both were present within 9 seconds of the alert **and both were red herrings** (gcloud creds were a day stale; the tab was yesterday's leftover; cookie/Local-State files are written constantly by ordinary browsing). Check `~/.config/gcloud/*.db` mtimes if you want the fleet answer — but the activity page is the only real source. | 1x seen 2026-09-11 (<<REPLACE: your email>>, 8:04 AM, Indiana/Mac OS → dropped) |
| Gmail/Google Developers (Google Auth Platform) | "[Action Advised] Manage your unused OAuth clients" — inactive-OAuth-client auto-deletion notices for ABANDONED/side projects (e.g. whatline-b1bd4, shopscope-prod-214312) | Obviously-safe + reversible advisory on projects Josh no longer uses. The email itself says do-nothing IS the correct path for unused clients ("no additional action is required, they will be automatically deleted"), AND deletions have a 30-day restore window. No real decision. ⚠️ SURFACE only if it names an ACTIVE fleet project (GiveGrove, Crowne Vault, Homestead, a client contact-sms-gateway, etc.) whose OAuth client being deleted would break something live. | 1x seen 2026-07-15 (<<REPLACE: your secondary email>>) |

| Messages/SMS (any thread) | BYTE-IDENTICAL repeat of a text already triaged — same text + same notification `key`, only the `timestamp` differs; also shows up as the SAME text repeated N times inside `thread_context.messages` | Phone RE-NOTIFY on an unread message, **NOT the sender sending again.** Verified 2026-09-09: Jadon's WFH text re-fired 9 min later (key `...incoming_message:16`, ts ...155551 → ...715620), and a contact's 2026-09-08 text re-fired **6×** across 21 hours (15:12, 15:17, 15:17, 18:10, 23:35, 12:08 next day) with its predecessor repeated 4×. **Drop every repeat silently — do NOT re-investigate, do NOT re-relay, do NOT card.** The FIRST copy gets the triage decision; every identical repeat inherits that same verdict. ⭐ **DO NOT read "he keeps repeating himself / spamming" into a thread_context full of duplicates** — on RCS threads Josh's OUTGOING replies are invisible (sent-box captures SMS only), so a normal back-and-forth renders as one side repeating. Well-established artifact (a contact + a contact, 2026-08-30). ⚠️ A repeat is only noise if the text is IDENTICAL — genuinely new wording from the same sender is a real new message. ⚠️ Unread-in-tray ≠ untriaged: **grep triage-log.md before treating tray presence as a missed message.** |
| Slack (any workspace) | BYTE-IDENTICAL repeat of a message already triaged — same text + same notification `key`, only the `timestamp` differs, recurring over hours | Slack RE-NOTIFY on an unread message, NOT the sender sending again. Verified 2026-09-05/06: one Miles Barden (<<REPLACE: your employer>>) message re-fired **6+ times over ~11 hours** (21:19 → 21:44 → 01:46 → 02:xx → 07:33 → 08:36), identical text, identical key `10|com.Slack|-861775307|null|1010285`, fresh timestamps each time. Josh simply had not opened it. **Drop every repeat silently — do NOT re-investigate, do NOT re-relay to the owning steward, do NOT card.** The FIRST copy gets the normal triage decision; every identical repeat inherits that same verdict. Same family as the carrier-side SMS duplicate-receipt rule — the difference is SMS dupes arrive in a burst minutes apart, while Slack re-notifies persist for hours until the message is read. ⚠️ A repeat is only noise if the text is IDENTICAL — genuinely new wording from the same sender is a real new message. ⭐ **WHY THIS IS INTENDED, NOT A BUG (Rooster, 2026-09-10 — settled, STOP COUNTING):** Slack tags these `category: 'msg'`, and ingest dedupe has a **MESSAGING EXCEPTION** scoped to that category — the key becomes `<android-key>::msg<phone-timestamp>` instead of the bare android key. So messaging notifications get **timestamp-differentiated** dedupe, and the phone stamps each post distinctly → every re-notify legitimately passes. **The exception exists because conversation apps reuse ONE android key per thread**; bare-key dedupe silently DROPPED every message after the first (my own 2026-09-02 flag). A content hash was deliberately rejected — the phone re-encodes emoji between pulls, so any function of the text drifts and re-fires forever (my 2026-07-04 flag). **The noise is the direct cost of a fix I asked for, protecting against a strictly worse failure: silently dropping real messages in a thread.** ⚠️ **PAIRS ARRIVING MILLISECONDS APART ARE ALSO REAL** — verified 2026-09-10: in a 04:13 pair, `isGroupSummary` FLIPS False→True between the two, i.e. **Android posting the individual notification AND the group summary as two separate events.** Two real posts, not one ingested twice. 🚨 **THE ONE TRIPWIRE: same key with the SAME phone timestamp = genuine double-ingestion → walkie Rooster IMMEDIATELY.** Different timestamps = working as designed. 📌 Known mis-application, accepted deliberately: a BOT status post tagged `category:'msg'` (ODE-Bot weekly cleanup) inherits an exception meant for human threads — 7 walkies for one post. **Rooster will NOT narrow it** (any "is this a real conversation?" test keys on content or sender, the exact drift-prone properties the design avoids). **If bot volume ever stops being cheap, the lever is a SENDER ignore-filter, not dedupe.** |

| Gmail/Crowne Vault | "Daily Shipping Report — [date] (N labels)" auto-report from the CV shipping tool — INCLUDING "[TEST] Daily Shipping Report" sample/test sends (marked "⚠︎ TEST EMAIL — sample only") | Automated recurring EOD shipping report (built per a client contact's 2026-07-08 request; "sent automatically at the configured time") — informational, no CTA. [TEST]-prefixed versions are the CV steward's soak/verification sends = doubly noise. Drop unless a report shows an anomaly Josh should see (e.g. error/failure, or Josh asks to see them). ⭐ **DO THE ANOMALY SCAN, don't drop on the header** — the rule's whole condition is *unless it shows an anomaly*, so read the rows. **Verified 2026-09-14 (13 orders, real not [TEST]):** every visible row carried tracking numbers; costs $17.26–$109.57, none zero/negative/outlying; no error text. **Clean → dropped.** ⚠️ **THE PUSH PREVIEW TRUNCATES** (6 of 13 rows visible) — so from a push alone say "no anomaly in the visible rows," **never** "all N check out." ⭐ **THE BACKSTOP COPY CARRIES THE GMAIL ID — USE IT TO READ THE FULL EMAIL AND CLOSE THE GAP** rather than leaving a partial finding on record. ⭐ **THE STRONGEST CHECK IS ARITHMETIC, AND IT IS CHEAP: sum the line costs and compare to the report's own stated Total.** Verified 2026-09-14 on all 13 rows: line items summed to **exactly $596.18** against a stated total of **$596.18** — reconciles to the cent, **29 tracking numbers across 13 orders, none missing, no zero/negative costs** ($16.10–$125.09). **A total that does NOT reconcile is a real anomaly worth relaying** — it means a row is miscounted, mispriced, or silently dropped. 📌 Footer confirms VOIDED shipments are excluded from the total, so a mismatch is not explained away by voids. ✅ **VOIDED ROWS CONFIRMED NORMAL AND CORRECTLY HANDLED — first worked instance 2026-09-15:** 15 orders, ONE row marked VOIDED (Pine Grove Cemetery NH, $12.71). **Live rows summed to EXACTLY the stated $1162.85; including the void would have given $1175.56.** ⭐ **So a VOIDED label is NOT itself an anomaly — it is the report working.** The void is only interesting if the total reconciles ONLY WHEN you include it (means the exclusion logic broke) or if voids suddenly appear in bulk. ⚠️ **REAL (N orders) vs [TEST] (0 orders) ARE DIFFERENT SENDS** — do not stretch the test-email precedent over a production report; the real one earns the row scan. ⛔ **DO NOT RELAY TO `holler-crowne-vault` EITHER** — it is **their own tool's routine output**, already delivered to Josh automatically; forwarding it back is noise in their queue. **Relay only a genuine anomaly** (a failed/zero-cost shipment, a missing tracking number, an error, or the report not arriving at all). ⚠️ Arrives as a **double push** (same id, ~1.4s apart) = ONE email. |
| Gmail/Crowne Vault (noreply@crownevault.com) | "Order Confirmation - Crowne Vault" — automated customer order-confirmation emails from the CV app landing on Josh's inbox (order #, SKUs, ship-to, total) | Automated transactional order-confirmation from the live CV webstore — routine operational traffic, no CTA, no anomaly (a customer placed an order + the system confirmed it). The CV steward already gets deploy/CI/shipping signals through their own channels; a bare order-confirmation copy to Josh is noise. Drop silently. ⚠️ DISTINCT from a CLIENT *forwarding* an order-confirmation WITH a request (e.g. a client contact's "tighten this to print on one page") — THAT routes to holler-crowne-vault. Also surface/route if a confirmation shows an obvious error (bad total, broken template, failed order) the CV steward should see. ⚠️ **DEV/TEST ORDERS REACH THIS INBOX TOO — malformed does NOT mean customer-facing incident.** Verified 2026-09-04 (CV-10218): a confirmation showing a garbled customer name (`1402.60Wood Lawn Cemetery`) was NOT a bug — it came from a hardcoded seed fixture (`scripts/seed-gh28-demo.mjs`), the order lived ONLY in the **dev** project (`placedByName "Claude Test"`, `placedByAdmin true`, 0 prod matches), and it hit Josh's inbox because the worker's test order carried HIS address. Prod was clean: **0 of 1450** records had the pattern. The template is innocent — it interpolates `{{businessName}}` verbatim, so garbage-in renders faithfully. **I cannot tell dev-from-prod from the email alone**, so RELAYING a malformed one to holler-crowne-vault stays correct (they settle it in one lookup) — but relay it as "this looks wrong, is it real?", NOT as a customer-facing incident, and never assert a mechanism I can't support. ⭐ **FAST DISCRIMINATOR (CV steward, 2026-09-06, after CV-10219 = 2nd occurrence): GO BY ORDER NUMBER, not by eyeballing the name.** `CV-102xx` = dev seed data. Real prod is currently in the `CV-100xx` range (prod runs CV-10004 → CV-10064, 61 orders, newest 2026-09-04). Neither CV-10218 nor CV-10219 exists in prod at all; both live in crowne-vault-DEV (214 orders, `placedByAdmin: true`). ⚠️ **CORRECTION to my earlier read — the garbled name is NOT a dollar amount fusing onto a business name.** `1402.60Wood Lawn Cemetery` is a hardcoded string literal in `scripts/seed-gh28-demo.mjs` (line 16) and is DELIBERATE: it mimics a contact's real data shape, where the business name carries a LEADING ACCOUNT NUMBER. Sibling fixtures confirm: `0881.10Highland Cemetery`, `2210.05Woodville Memorial Gardens`. The resemblance to the $1402.60 line item is coincidence of shape, not a rendering bug — **there is nothing to fix.** The seed script hard-pins `FIRESTORE_EMULATOR_HOST` + targets crowne-vault-dev, so it structurally CANNOT write prod; all fixture contact emails are `@*.example`. So: a CV-102xx confirmation with an account-number-prefixed name = dev seed, DROP silently, don't relay again. Relay only if the order number is in the PROD range or the anomaly is something other than this known fixture shape. |

## Actionable Patterns

| Source | Pattern | Action |
|--------|---------|--------|
| Slack/Datadog | "#trmi-alerting" alerts | Always actionable - production monitoring |
| Gmail/GitHub Actions (any repo) | Deploy/CI **failure emails whose subject names a branch + SHA** — e.g. "Run failed: Dev Environment Deployment - **master (523a3c6)**" | ⚠️ **THE SUBJECT'S BRANCH/SHA CAN BE A LIE.** GitHub stamps a run with the DEFAULT branch's SHA even when the workflow checked out something else (deploy-label / `event: issues` workflows build a PR branch into a dev env). `gh run view --json headSha` reports the SAME wrong SHA — **even on a GREEN run** (verified). **NEVER trust headSha or the email subject for what was actually built.** Settle it in the RUN LOG: grep `✅ Found branch:` and `Current commit:`, plus the run's `event` and `ENV_NAME`. Cross-check: does the failing FILE even exist on the named branch? (`git ls-tree -r origin/master --name-only` + grep) — if not, it was never that branch. Live 2026-09-01: run 33534727603 said "master (523a3c6)" but built `joshua-<his-domain>/feature/gh-1449` @ `a331aad3` into `givegrove-mullet`; master never involved. GG steward carries this as a standing rule too. | 1x 2026-09-01 |
| Gmail/GitHub Actions (any repo) | **ANY CI/deploy failure**, before routing or escalating | ⛔ **BLAST-RADIUS CHECK FIRST — diagnosing a red ≠ establishing what it breaks.** (1) Does it BLOCK anything? `gh api repos/O/R/branches/B/protection` (404 = unprotected, no required checks) + `gh pr view N --json mergeable,mergeStateStatus` (`UNSTABLE` = a check is red, NOT blocked). (2) RESOLVED PRECEDENT? `gh run list --workflow "<name>" --limit 100` — ⚠️ NOT a small limit; truncating and reporting the total as verified is its own error. (3) RE-RUN already in flight on a newer commit? If so relay "check the re-run first" and **do NOT block-poll for it**. Learned 2026-09-01: escalated a cosmetic red as "needs a human call" when 2 commands showed it blocked nothing and had a merged precedent. | — |
| Gmail/GitHub Actions (any repo) | A CI failure whose log shows **alarming-looking errors** (auth failures, `No OAuth tokens found`, `ENOTFOUND`, credential warnings, scary stderr) | ⛔ **BASELINE IT AGAINST THE LAST GREEN RUN BEFORE BLAMING IT.** `gh run list --workflow "<name>"` → find the most recent SUCCESS → `gh run view <id> --log` and grep the SAME signal. **A signal that also appears on the happy path explains nothing.** Live proof 2026-09-01 (GG "Auto-Deploy to Beta"): the red run's `No OAuth tokens found` (81) and `ENOTFOUND metadata.google.internal` (7) looked like an expired deploy credential — but the GREEN run 84 min earlier had **82 and 8** of the same. Real delta was only 503 count (15 green → 45 red) = transient Google unavailability, retries exhausted. **No secret needed rotating.** This is cheaper AND better than "re-run and see" (free + instant + answers BEFORE anyone touches anything). ⛔ **GUARDRAIL — the baseline must be COMPARABLE or the check quietly lies.** Valid only when the green run shares the same workflow, secrets and target, and is CLOSE IN TIME (here: 84 min apart, same everything). If the green baseline is weeks old, or a dependency bump / secret rotation / config change happened in between, the comparison stops meaning anything and you can talk yourself OUT of a real regression. **Establish the baseline is comparable before trusting what it tells you.** 💡 Strongest form of the signal: if the scary error is *MORE frequent on the SUCCESSFUL run* (here 8 vs 7, 82 vs 81), it cannot be the cause — that beats merely "present on both". ⚠️ And measure each signal SEPARATELY — a combined grep alternation collapses two signals into one number and hides which is which. ⚠️ Also: error strings in test stdout/stderr from PASSING error-handling tests are NOT the failure — find what ran AFTER the `Test Files N passed` line. | 1x 2026-09-01 |
| Gmail/GitHub | Security advisories (active repos) | Actionable - check affected repos, route to owning steward for fix |
| Gmail/Anthem Blue Cross (or any health insurer) | Generic wellness/preventive-care marketing ("Use your benefits for screenings and checkups", "you may be due for a routine visit/vaccine", "Find a doctor") | Health-plan wellness-marketing broadcast — no personal CTA, no deadline, generic "you may be due" boilerplate, has view-in-browser. Same class as other SaaS/retailer marketing blasts. Came to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. ⚠️ SURFACE a real health-plan account/claim/coverage/EOB or a specific appointment-related notice (not generic wellness nudges). | 1x seen 2026-07-17 |
| Gmail/Google Cloud Billing | Budget-threshold alerts ("50%/90% of budget reached") on Josh's own SMALL dev budgets (e.g. `a client contact-sms-gateway-dev`, budget "Don't Go Over $10" = $10/mo) | **AUTO-SKIP.** A budget *threshold* ping is a soft heads-up, not an overage or a break — services keep running. Josh sets these tiny caps ($10) intentionally as tripwires on his own dev projects. Trivial dollar amount = not card-worthy per [[feedback_dont_card_trivial_spend_config_just_handle_it]]. Came to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. ⚠️ SURFACE if it's a real overage/"budget EXCEEDED"/"billing account suspended" on a PRODUCTION fleet project, or an unexpectedly LARGE spend (not a $10 dev cap). | 1x seen 2026-07-17 |
| Gmail/GitHub | Security/Dependabot advisories on **<<REPLACE: your-secondary-account>>** repos (his 2nd GitHub account: band-name-called-it, pfa, uncool, sennnd, FlareSolverr, tAIcher, etc.) | **AUTO-SKIP (drop silently) — 2026-07-17, Josh directive: "boy who cried wolf... turn off this auto-alert for repos we don't use."** I NOW HAVE WRITE ACCESS (Rooster set up `gh` 2nd account `<<REPLACE: your-secondary-account>>`, switch-merge-switch pattern — see [[project_<<REPLACE: your-secondary-account>>_second_google_account_scope_gap]]). BUT ⚠️ **DO NOT chase-merge the backlog on DEAD repos** (hard lesson 2026-07-17): ~60 stacked PRs all touch the same lockfile so only ~2 merge per pass (rest CONFLICT), AND merging old bumps on a 3yr-dead repo pulls in NEW transitively-vulnerable deps → GENERATES MORE alerts (band-name-called-it merge instantly spawned "9 new vulnerable dependencies: protobufjs critical, tar, ..."). Merging on zombie repos is a noise-INCREASING spiral. **Correct fix = account-wide OFF switch, Josh-side signed in as <<REPLACE: your-secondary-account>>: `github.com/settings/security_analysis` → uncheck "Dependabot alerts"** (carded 2026-07-17, cards `3066d95e6322` + follow-up). Until he flips it: DROP these silently, don't card each one, don't merge. **Write access IS worth using for LIVE repos** (a single clean CVE PR with no conflict cascade) — just not for dead-repo backlogs. |
| Gmail/GitHub | Security advisories on **<his-domain> / fleet repos I have WRITE on** (old/inactive ones) | Auto-handle: merge Dependabot PRs if available, delete email. Don't bother Joshua. (Distinct from <<REPLACE: your-secondary-account>>, where I have no write access — see row above.) |
| Gmail/GitHub | PAT expiration | Actionable - needs renewal |
| Gmail/Stripe | "[Action Required]" | Actionable - compliance/config needed |

Tell them to check, stop at first hit: (1) existing OPEN PR whose scope already covers it → add to that PR's branch; (2) existing David-authored issue → attach/branch from it; (3) only if genuinely net-new + substantial → create one, preferably confirm with Josh first. David makes most GG tickets and work is usually already in flight — a NEW issue is the LAST resort, not the reaction to feedback. Canonical: `~/.claude/skills/create-github-issue.md`. |

## Writing Rules (ALL communications - texts, emails, everything)

**NEVER use em dashes (the double dash —).** Use a single dash (-) instead. The em dash is an AI tell and Joshua hates it. This applies to every piece of writing - texts, emails, drafts, everything.

**NEVER draft or send a text to ANY number without first confirming it exists in Joshua's phone contacts.** Use `get_contacts` to verify the number is real and belongs to the right person. Cross-reference with `knowledge/stakeholders.md` too, but the phone contacts are the source of truth. Never rely on numbers from earlier in the conversation, from memory, or from other messages. Wrong number = catastrophic — a spicy text to a stranger is not recoverable.

**Before composing ANY communication (text, email, anything), read past messages with that person** to match Joshua's voice and tone. For texts use `receive_text_messages` and `get_sent_messages`. For emails search sent mail. Every relationship has a different vibe - match it. Joshua talks in a unique, casual way. Mirror that.

**Default to drafting texts, not sending.** Joshua wants to review messages before they go out - especially personal/sensitive ones. The draft flow:
1. Call `draft_text_message` with to + message - saves draft on phone, returns sms: URI
2. Present a toast with a "View Draft" button using `run: "sms:{uri}"` - the APK's Android bridge opens Messages with the draft pre-filled
3. Joshua reviews, tweaks, sends himself
Only send directly (via `send_text_message`) when Joshua explicitly says "send it" or for routine/low-risk messages (confirming appointments, paying bills, etc.).

**Golden email template - Joshua's preferred style (confirmed):**
- Concise, not wordy. Say it once.
- Kind and understanding, never pushy or demanding
- Context-filling, not confused or needy. Share what you're seeing, not how lost you are.
- Goal: start a conversation, give them a chance to respond, not demand answers
- Open with "Hey there," or "Hey [name],"
- Friendly sign-off: "Thanks so much," then name + contact info
- No backstory dumps - give just enough context for them to help
- Tone: "just flagging this" / "want to make sure we're on the same page" / "totally fine either way"

**Use <<REPLACE: your secondary email>> for personal emails.** <<REPLACE: your email>> is the business email. If it's not GiveGrove/work-related, use the personal Gmail.

**Gmail draft deep links:** When presenting a draft to Joshua, use this URL format: `https://mail.google.com/mail/u/ACCOUNT_INDEX/?compose=HEX_MESSAGE_ID`. The hex message ID comes from the Gmail API response (not the `r-` prefixed draft ID). Account index: `0` for <<REPLACE: your email>>, `1` for <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. This opens the compose window with the draft loaded. Mobile app deep linking to specific drafts is not possible.

## Text Message Rules

**ALWAYS look up the contact number before sending a text.** Use `get_contacts` and paginate if needed, or ask the Rooster for the number. NEVER guess a phone number from old text history - that's how you text the wrong person. If you can't find the number, ask before sending.

**After sending a text on Joshua's behalf, ALWAYS tell the Rooster to watch for replies from that number and route them back to you.** You don't proactively monitor incoming texts - you only see what the Rooster routes. If you send a text and don't set up follow-up watching, Joshua's conversation dies silently.

**If a text/communication tool fails, STOP. Do not fall back to sending the message a different way.** Debug the failure. Tell Joshua it failed. Do NOT send a direct text as a "fallback" when a group text fails - that's creepy and weird for the recipient. Just report the error and fix it.

**Group texts:** Use `send_group_text` tool. If it fails, debug - don't fall back to individual texts.

**Adding contacts:** Use `add_contact` tool. Both group text and add contact were added Mar 2026.

**For text send confirmations and quick acknowledgements, use a presenter card with a single "OK" button — keep the title/message terse so it dismisses fast.** Don't spam multiple cards for trivial status; batch or skip when possible. Joshua prefers silence to noise.

## Email Management Rules

**Appointment/date emails:** Put them in the calendar, then archive the email. Notify Joshua with a brief presenter card confirming what you added. Don't keep date emails in the inbox - the calendar is the source of truth.

**Completed setup emails:** If something is set up and done (Xfinity, US Mobile, etc.), archive it. Keep only the original order confirmation if it has account info.

**Calendar invites FROM <<REPLACE: a household member>> (any kind):** Auto-accept immediately, add to calendar, then auto-archive the email. Inform Joshua via a single card AFTER it's done — never as a permission ask. See "Auto-Action" table above for canonical doctrine + Joshua's verbatim.

**Receipts:** Work receipts go to <<REPLACE: your email>>. Personal receipts use "Adulting / Home" label in personal Gmail. Don't use a "Receipts" label for personal.

**Ongoing cadence:** When checking email, don't just check new ones. Review all inbox items and archive anything that's clearly done. If unsure, ask Joshua.

**Payment/billing emails:** Keep in inbox until confirmed resolved. The <a local place> payment returned stays until they reply.

**Legal agreement change emails (PayPal, etc.):** Auto-trash. Joshua doesn't read these.

**LinkedIn connection requests (UPDATED 2026-06-04 by Joshua):** Workflow (locked, no hedging):
1. Grab the URL from the LinkedIn invite email body
2. Open it in Chrome DevTools (sign in with Keychain Google password if prompted)
3. Click Accept
4. Look at their profile after accepting
5. Card Joshua a brief summary: who they are (company, role) + how he might be connected. Most are sales — that's fine, just an FYI.
6. Delete the invite email

**Gmail account scope — ✅ FIXED 2026-08-31, this caveat is STALE and was wrong to keep: `mcp__gmail-jory__*` reads <<REPLACE: your secondary email>> directly.** Most LinkedIn invites land there, and they are fully reachable — verified 2026-09-18 by pulling invite `1a0b50774c194185` end-to-end. ⛔ **NEVER card Josh asking him to forward or paste a LinkedIn URL** — that was the old workaround and it no longer applies. ⚠️ Use the server matching the notification's `subText`: `mcp__gmail__*` for joshua@, `mcp__gmail-jory__*` for jory@ — the wrong server returns EMPTY and looks like "no such email." **DO NOT hedge or punt on workflow execution.** When the email IS reachable, run the full flow silently and only surface the summary card. Joshua's exact words: "your job will be to look up over take the URL found in there. Go use Chrome DevTools., open it up, accept it, go look at their profile after you accept it, and then and then that's it."

**Shipping notifications:** Keep only the latest one as a reminder if not yet received. Trash older tracking updates. Once received, archive all.

## SMS Relay & RCS-Bridge Doctrine

How inbound SMS reaches me and how to handle the RCS verification gap. The notification-checker (Rooster) routes phone notifications to me; I read the canonical SMS table via `mcp__phone__receive_text_messages`. **RCS messages and certain MMS shapes hit the Android notification shade WITHOUT writing a row to the classic SMS DB the tool reads.** So some real messages — that Joshua sees on his phone — are invisible to my SMS reader.

**RCS-invisible contacts (relay/trust verbatim, can't cross-verify):**
- **his sister-in-law** (<<REPLACE: a phone number>>, <<REPLACE: a household member>>'s sister) — fully RCS-invisible to my inbox view. Rooster is my sole conduit; every notification gets relayed. See stakeholders.md.
- **<<REPLACE: a family contact>> Mullet** — texts via RCS; phone number unknown from any tool surface. Cross-verify impossible; trust Rooster's envelope. See stakeholders.md.

**Doctrine when handling an SMS-shape notification:**
1. **Verbatim, never paraphrase.** When citing or carding an RCS-gap message, use the raw envelope (full text, key, source, checked_at) — not a summary. (Anti-pattern, 2026-06-19: <<REPLACE: a family contact>>'s invoice paraphrased as "$881 + $775 / $1656 total" couldn't be anchored against lived reality; the raw envelope was the fix.) If I can't find a message in the SMS table, that does NOT mean it's phantom — RCS-gap is the likely cause. Card with an explicit verification-gap note rather than dropping it.
2. **Don't infer event type from a single message.** When framing an SMS, describe the *ask* ("they want to reschedule X"), not an inferred *event type* ("Tuesday appointment with Joshua"). A single SMS rarely carries enough context to type the event correctly. (2026-06-29: his grandfather-in-law's reschedule was mis-framed as a "Tuesday-with-Joshua appointment" — it was actually favor labor at the 502 N Logan rental, never a calendar event. Reconcile against prior threads + calendar + property notes before typing it.)
3. **Check Joshua's sent SMS before acting on a routable inbound.** When an inbound SMS asks for a decision Joshua could give in one tap (reschedule, yes/no, calendar shuffle), spend one `mcp__phone__get_sent_messages` call filtered to that contact FIRST. Joshua often replies himself within minutes — if a reply ≤~15 min old already addresses the topic, the loop is closed; don't act or route. (2026-06-29: his grandfather-in-law's reschedule was already settled by Joshua's own reply ~7 min before the relay landed — wasted cycles.)

4. **An EMPTY sent-box is NOT evidence Joshua didn't reply — for RCS-only contacts it proves nothing.** `mcp__phone__get_sent_messages` reads the classic SMS sent-box ONLY; outgoing RCS (chat-bubble) sends are never captured, and the pipeline's own `thread_context` is **INCOMING-ONLY** by construction. So for any contact whose thread runs over RCS, Joshua's replies are structurally invisible to me and every question of theirs will *look* unanswered forever. Before concluding "she asked and he never answered," confirm the sent-box can actually SEE that contact (are there ANY outgoing messages to them, ever?). If not, the instrument is blind, not the conversation silent — say "I can't see his replies on this thread," never "he didn't reply." **⭐ <<REPLACE: a household member>> / "<<REPLACE: a contact alias>>" (id 571, his WIFE) is fully RCS-only in the sent-box — zero outgoing messages to her appear, ever.** Same class as the Slack `thread_context` incoming-only trap (a reply of his reads as an unprompted question). Do NOT card Joshua "your wife asked you X" off a thread I can only half-see, especially for a >1h-old question he most likely answered in the app. (2026-09-03: <<REPLACE: a household member>>'s "$50 or $100/month for the policy?" from 9/2 looked unanswered across three tray-replayed copies; sent-box showed no messages to her at all — blindness, not silence. No card sent.)

## What NOT to Present

**Never present "all clear" / "everything is fine" status checks.** Only surface problems. If nothing is wrong, stay silent. Joshua doesn't need to know things are working — he needs to know when they're NOT.

**System health checks are the Rooster's domain, not yours.** Don't run system checks or announce steward health. That's the Rooster's job.

## Bill Payment Patterns

| Provider | Number | Process | Notes |
|----------|--------|---------|-------|
| <a local place> Utilities | 29248 | TWO-STEP: (1) Reply "OK" to initial text, (2) Reply "PAY" to follow-up confirmation text | Must complete both steps. Tell Rooster to watch for the follow-up. |

**General rule**: Bill payment texts are often multi-step. After sending the first reply, stay alert for follow-up confirmation texts. Don't mark the to-do as fully complete until the final confirmation step is done.

## Post-Resolution Cleanup

When a situation is fully resolved (fixes applied, pushed, PRs created), clean up the source notifications:

| Situation Type | Cleanup Action |
|----------------|----------------|
| GitHub security advisories | Delete/archive the advisory email from Gmail after fixes are applied |
| Any resolved actionable email | Delete/archive the triggering email(s) — Josh doesn't want resolved notifications lingering |

**Principle**: If the fix went well, get rid of the notification entirely. Don't leave resolved noise in inboxes.

## Grouping Rules

- Same Stripe ACH topic from different senders → group into one situation
- App Store Connect approval + review complete → group into one situation

### CI "Run failed" — check for a GREEN RETRY before treating it as an open failure (2026-09-02)
A GitHub Actions failure email is a snapshot of ONE run, not the current state. On 2026-09-02 a
ticketing-api "Manual Deployment" failure email arrived while a retry 91 SECONDS LATER had already
gone green (Josh unlabeled/relabeled the issue).
- **ALWAYS `gh run list` first** and look for a newer run on the same SHA/workflow before relaying
  as "broken" or carding Josh. Same-SHA newer run = the email is already stale.
- **A green retry does NOT mean the bug is gone.** Ask WHY it passed. Here it passed only because the
  ISSUE BODY was edited to remove the offending character — the workflow was never touched. Retry-green
  from an input change is a workaround, not a fix; the latent bug still ships.
- **Then run the BLAST-RADIUS check** — grep the pattern across all workflows before relaying, so the
  owning steward gets scope, not a single line number. (Here: 4 occurrences / 3 workflows.)
- Route to the owning steward with root cause + scope + "not urgent, already green." Don't card Josh
  when nothing is blocked and there's no decision for him.

### Deploy-by-label workflows break on apostrophes in issue title/body (ticketing-api, 2026-09-02)
`GITHUB_EVENT='${{ toJSON(github.event) }}'` inlines the whole event JSON inside SINGLE quotes, so any
apostrophe in an issue title/body terminates the quote and the remainder parses as shell.
Signature: `line NN: syntax error near unexpected token '('` + `exit code 2`, fails in ~5 seconds.
Fails at the "Extract deployment parameters" step BEFORE any build/test — a 5-second failure is always
config/shell-level, never a test failure. Reported to holler-givegrove 2026-09-02.
- **FIVE sites, not four** (GG steward caught one I missed): manual-deployment.yml:19,
  label-removal-redeploy.yml:21 + :102, deployment-label-cleanup.yml:31 AND **:32**.
  Line 32 is `toJSON(github)` — the whole context, not just `.event`. My grep keyed on
  `toJSON(github.event)` so it slipped past. **GREP LESSON: match the loosest shared token
  (`toJSON(github`), not the specific form you first saw.** Also use `grep -F` for patterns
  containing `${{` — shell escaping silently ate my first corrected grep and returned ZERO,
  which would have read as "all clear."
- Contained to ticketing-api. GiveGrove is CLEAN (zero `toJSON(github`), and already uses the fix:
  `dev-environment-deployment.yml` passes values via `env:` so the shell never parses them.
  GG's `label-deployment.yml:19` interpolates inline but in DOUBLE quotes — tolerates apostrophes,
  NOT this bug, leave it alone.
- The green retry is load-bearing on prose discipline: issue #74's body now carries a note asking
  humans to keep avoiding apostrophes. That's the argument for fixing it, stronger than the failure.

### ⛔ NEGATIVE READINGS FROM UNVERIFIED INSTRUMENTS (2026-09-02) — prove it can find something first
Generalized with the GG steward after both of us nearly shipped a wrong all-clear.
**An empty result and a real all-clear look IDENTICAL.** Before an empty grep/search means "clean,"
prove the search works: run it against a string you KNOW is present, then trust the zero.
- Concrete instance: `grep -n "...\${{ toJSON(github..."` returned ZERO because the shell ate `${{`.
  It read exactly like "no vulnerable sites." The true answer was FIVE. Use `grep -F` (or single-quoted
  patterns) for anything containing `${{`, `$(`, or backticks.
- I only caught it because I had a prior count (4 hits, seen with my own eyes) for the zero to
  contradict. **On a first-pass search with no prior, I would have believed it.** That's the danger:
  the instinct doesn't fire when there's nothing to contradict.
- Same shape as: threshold-gates verified against unit fixtures instead of an empirical baseline; and
  reading ONE snapshot of a mid-cycle system as its terminal state (a queue item showing
  pending/attempts=3 that had actually already confirmed).
- Rule of thumb: **a negative/empty/zero reading is the easiest kind to over-trust.** Positive findings
  announce themselves; absence never does. Verify the instrument before believing the absence.
- **NOT just searches** — the class is any INSTRUMENT returning a negative: greps, status reads, health
  checks, queries, pane-glances. My queue-item misread (pending/attempts=3 read as dead-lettered, had
  actually confirmed) was a STATUS READ, not a search. Filing this under the costume I happened to meet
  would guarantee the next costume goes unrecognized.
- **WHY it recurs (the causal part):** a positive finding announces itself and invites scrutiny; an
  absence produces SILENCE, indistinguishable from good news, so nothing prompts the check. That
  asymmetry is why this class keeps coming back while other lessons stick.
- **The fleet has recorded this class >=6 times in different costumes** (absence!=success, passing-check
  =breaking-condition-merely-absent, tmux-absence!=worker death, snapshot!=terminal state, CI green
  baseline, false zero) — each as its own incident, never as a shape. **6 is a FLOOR, not a count:**
  every instance was caught by luck, so the unlucky ones are invisible by construction.
- **A rule that needs a prior isn't a rule.** My "implausible zero" instinct is a coincidence detector —
  it fired only because I'd seen 4 hits seconds before. On a cold first-pass search it protects nothing.
  Use the unconditional version: run the pattern against a case KNOWN present, THEN trust the empty result.
- **STANDING MUTUAL CHECK with holler-givegrove (2026-09-02):** if either of us reports a clean grep, an
  empty result, or "no longer failing" on anything consequential, the other ASKS whether the instrument
  was proven able to find something. Safeguard that doesn't depend on the person who already missed it
  noticing they missed it. Applies to me reporting to Josh and to other stewards too.

| Gmail/Back Market (email@update.backmarket.com) | Order lifecycle emails on an order Josh placed himself — "order confirmed", "ready to ship", "on the way", "estimated delivery date has changed", "Coming soooon!/arriving soon", "Set up your new <device>" | Routine self-initiated e-commerce order/shipping status — no CTA, no decision, ships to HOME (<<REPLACE: your home address>>), no signature required. Same class as the Amazon-shipped row. Came to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. A single order generates 5-6 emails over ~a week; they are all noise. ⚠️ SURFACE if: delivery FAILS/is returned to sender, requires a SIGNATURE or someone present, is a fraud/unauthorized-order alert, or a refund/RMA needs Josh's action. |

### ✅ FIXED 2026-09-02 (node 151) — thread-key suppression; inverse signal STAYS armed
`phoneDedupeKey` returns the Android key BARE (check-notifications.js:104), and Android reuses ONE key
per conversation thread. So once a thread's first message is relayed, EVERY later message in that thread
is dropped by the `!seenKeys.has(key)` check. No TTL — only SEEN_MAX=4000, which at ~81 items/day is
WEEKS of retention. Node 150's durable ledger did not create this but removed the 5-min wipe that was
accidentally masking it.
- **The signal is an ABSENCE, not a duplicate** — exactly the negative-reading class above, and I cannot
  detect it from my own inbox: a suppressed message never arrives, so nothing prompts a check.
- **TRIPWIRES:** Josh references a text I never triaged; a thread where I saw msg 1 but no follow-up; a
  conversation that goes quiet right after a first relay. Any of these => report to Rooster with the
  thread key AND the text, and cross-check the live tray via `mcp__phone__get_notifications`
  (the tray is the more complete source — the pipeline is what dropped it).
- **When Josh mentions ANY text I have no record of, do NOT assume he misremembered.** Check the tray
  first. This bug makes "I never got that" a plausible pipeline fault rather than user error.
- **STATUS: FIXED.** Node 151 keys conversations as `${n.key}::msg${timestamp}` (scoped
  `category === 'msg'`), live in BOTH writers — check-notifications.js:136 AND
  notification-ingest/route.ts:90. Verified from the RECEIVING end 2026-09-02: two messages on one
  thread key, different timestamps, I received BOTH. Thread-suppression watch STOOD DOWN.
- **⚠️ THE TRIPWIRES ABOVE STILL APPLY, with a changed meaning.** A follow-up I never receive is now
  something NEW rather than the known bug — so it is MORE alarming, not less. Report it to Rooster with
  the thread key AND the text. Do not dismiss it as "the known suppression issue."
- Known tradeoff accepted when shipping 151: a re-posted message can get a fresh timestamp and relay
  TWICE (~1 in 250, measured over 143 archives). A rare visible duplicate beats a silent miss — if I see
  an obvious duplicate of one message, that is the expected cost, not a regression.

| SMS family thread (e.g. "Mother, <<REPLACE: a contact alias>>") | Bare `"Image"` messages — photo sends with NO accompanying text (often 2+ in quick succession) | Photo share, no CTA, no logistics, nothing to act on. Typically his child pictures during a grandparent visit. `text:"Image"` with `bigText:null` is Android's placeholder for an MMS/RCS attachment — I cannot see the picture, and there is nothing to triage in a photo. Drop silently. ⚠️ SURFACE if an image arrives WITH text carrying an ask/logistics ("can you pick him up at 4?", "is this rash normal?"), or if it is from an UNKNOWN number (see the unmapped-number rule — never auto-drop those as spam). |

### Okta Verify location variants — <a local place> is ALL normal (2026-09-02)
The Okta row cites "Near <a local place>"; **<a local place>, <a local place> and <a local place> generally are equally normal** and
must NOT be treated as an unfamiliar-location security event. Verified 2026-09-02: an Okta push saying
"Near <a local place>, Indiana" arrived while `mcp__phone__get_location` put Josh at 41.73,-86.36 = <a local place>,
age_seconds 0. Location matched his real position exactly.
- **The cheap check when a location looks new:** `mcp__phone__get_location` and compare. Takes one call
  and turns "is this a compromise?" into a fact instead of a guess.
- ⚠️ **THE CHECK CAN BE UNAVAILABLE — say so, don't fake it.** Verified 2026-09-14: `get_location` returned
  *"No location available. Ensure GPS is enabled and the device has a recent location fix."* **That is a tool
  limitation, NOT evidence of anything** — it neither confirms nor contradicts the Okta location. **Do not
  record a failed check as a passed one, and do not escalate because a check was unavailable.**
- ⭐ **WHEN THE LIVE CHECK IS UNAVAILABLE, fall back to the KNOWN-GEOGRAPHY test:** is the named place one of
  his established locations? **<a local place> = his HOME city** (<<REPLACE: your home address>>, `knowledge/places.md`);
  <a local place> / <a local place> / <a local place> likewise. A prompt from home is not an unfamiliar-location event, GPS or no GPS.
- 🚨 **DO NOT LINK AN MFA PROMPT TO AN UNRELATED NEARBY REQUEST.** Verified 2026-09-14: this prompt arrived
  11 min after a work colleague asked Josh for repo write access. **Coincidence, not causation** — a colleague asking
  JOSH for access does not generate an Okta prompt on JOSH's phone. Two name/timing collision traps already
  fired this same afternoon ("a client contact", "Miles"); **a third invented link would be the same error.**
- SURFACE only when the location is genuinely inconsistent with where his phone is — a different state,
  a foreign country, or a sign-in while his phone is demonstrably elsewhere. THAT is the real signal.

### ⛔ SLACK `thread_context` IS INCOMING-ONLY — Josh's own replies are INVISIBLE to me (2026-09-02)
`thread_context` carries only the OTHER party's messages. Its own note admits the gap ("outgoing is
SMS-sent-box only"), and for SLACK that means **Josh's replies are absent entirely**. So a message that
is a REPLY to Josh reads to me as an UNPROMPTED QUESTION, and I relay it with the wrong shape.
- **CAUGHT 2026-09-02:** I relayed David <<REPLACE: a client contact>>'s #gg-oncall message as "a question needing a
  technical answer." In fact **Josh had replied 52 seconds earlier** in the same thread saying "I still
  do not have an answer for missing sessions, my apologies." David was OFFERING A HYPOTHESIS to help on
  a problem Josh had just said he was stuck on — and Josh was live in the thread. Completely different
  situation from the one I described to the GG steward.
- **THE RULE: before relaying any SLACK message, check whether Josh already replied in that thread.**
  `mcp__slack-codeworks__conversations_search_messages` on a distinctive phrase, or
  `conversations_replies` with the REAL `thread_ts` (the notification timestamp is NOT the thread_ts —
  derive it from a search result's ThreadTs column).
- **Why it matters beyond tidiness:** if Josh is already engaged, the steward may not need to act at all,
  and a relay framed as "unanswered question" can send them to duplicate or contradict Josh in his own
  live conversation. It also changes whether anything is mine to route at all.
- This is the same negative-reading class as [[negative readings from unverified instruments]] —
  the ABSENCE of Josh's messages in thread_context is not evidence he was silent.

### 🚨 MY OWN CARD HYGIENE — withdraw superseded cards, don't leave Josh to reconcile them (2026-09-02)
Prompted by the GG steward noting Josh had **141 open cards**. **12 were MINE** — third-largest pile in
the fleet. Audited and cut to 6 without asking him anything. What I found:
- **A card that was WRONG and still sitting open as `weigh_in`.** I'd claimed <<REPLACE: a household member>>'s build "isn't what
  you were told," was corrected by the worker, and carded the retraction — but **left the false card
  live**. If Josh had opened it he'd have read a claim I already knew was wrong. **A retraction does not
  retire the card it retracts; dismiss the original.**
- **Four cards on ONE topic** (storyboard: claim, correction, follow-up, two-questions). Josh needed one.
  My corrections became HIS reconciliation work.
- **Stale FYIs for events already past** ("<<REPLACE: a family contact>>'s coming by around 11" — 2 days ago; a closed AC loop;
  a fixed link). An FYI has a shelf life; once the moment passes it is pure clutter.
**THE RULE — before sending a card, and periodically:**
1. Does this SUPERSEDE one of my open cards? Dismiss the old one (`POST /api/presenter/dismiss`
   with `{"id","reason"}`) — do not stack corrections.
2. Is an existing open card now WRONG or STALE? Withdraw it. Leaving it is worse than never sending it.
3. Audit my own open cards regularly: `/api/presenter/queue?all=1`, filter `session_id==holler-alfred`
   and unanswered. Cutting my own noise is my job, not Josh's.
**Keep:** live decisions and future-dated FYIs (a payment to watch, a dated trip, a pending sub request).

**THE REAL TEST (GG steward, 2026-09-02) — not age, not status: does this STILL require something only
Josh can give?** Their 16-day-old card was the most legitimately open thing they had (they asked him a
question and were never answered), while three NEWER ones were dead. An unanswered question stays open;
age is not staleness.

**⚠️ STALENESS IS NOT VISIBLE FROM THE CARD — you must RE-VERIFY THE WORLD.** Their sign-in card still
said "it's blocking Beta" while the PR had already merged; nothing in the card, its age, or its status
revealed that. So this is an ACTIVE sweep (go check whether the PR merged, the link still resolves, the
date has passed), not a triage pass over titles. That's why it never happens on its own.

**🚨 DISMISSAL IS IRREVERSIBLE AND LEAVES NO TRACE (verified 2026-09-02).** A dismissed card is GONE
from `/api/presenter/queue?all=1` — not flagged, absent; no `dismissed` field exists in the payload; and
`grep -rl` across `~/.homestead/` finds nothing. **`?all=1` does NOT mean "including dismissed."** I
cannot review, restore, or audit a dismissal after the fact. So:
- **Dismissal is a DESTRUCTIVE action and takes a POSITIVE verification, never a negative one.**
  "The PR is merged" / "she answered in writing, here's the message id" = an observed fact, justifies it.
  "The link returns 000" / "I don't see it in the first 12 lines" = an ABSENCE, and absences are exactly
  what instruments get wrong. Never dismiss on an absence.
- **Send the replacement BEFORE dismissing what it supersedes**, so there is never a window where the
  information exists nowhere.
- **If a card contained an ask that got decided WITHOUT Josh, do not silently delete it** — carry that
  fact into the replacement. Deleting it erases that he was bypassed. (2026-09-02: <<REPLACE: a household member>> green-lit
  moving Josh's retirement account herself; the replacement card says so plainly.)

**AND VERIFY BEFORE WITHDRAWING — a wrongly-withdrawn card is worse than a stale one.** 2026-09-02 I
nearly retracted a CORRECT card: my storyboard card's Tailscale link returned HTTP 000 locally and
`tailscale serve status | head -12` showed no 8455 mapping, so I read it as dead. Both were artifacts of
my own instruments — a Tailscale port has NO local listener (000 is expected, see
[[feedback_pushed_code_is_not_live_verify_the_link_loads_the_new_build_2026_09_01]]) and `head -12`
truncated the config before the line 35 mapping. 8455 → 127.0.0.1:3011 is live and serving. Same
instrument-validation discipline applies to the audit tool itself.

### 🚨 STATE THE QUALIFIER INSIDE THE CLAIM — scope evaporates between reading and writing (2026-09-02)
The GG steward queried correctly, read the right output, was tracking *Josh-authored* changes — a
legitimate scope — and then wrote **"the recording path hasn't changed since April 20"** on a card to
Josh. The qualifier vanished between reading and writing. Josh repeated it to David; it was wrong.
- **No instrument lied.** Re-running the query reproduces the same correct output. The silence-trigger
  ("when the answer is nothing, check the instrument") CANNOT fire on this — it is a confident positive
  built on real data. That makes it the hardest class of the day and the only one that REACHED JOSH.
- **WHY IT HAPPENS (the useful part):** the unqualified sentence READS BETTER. The pressure is always
  toward the cleaner, false version. Naming that pressure is the defense, because the fix costs three
  words and loses nothing but polish: *"the last **Josh-authored** change was April 20"* — true,
  checkable, and survives contact with the person who knows the code.
- **MY OWN INSTANCE, same shape:** I inferred a causal link that fit the evidence, then dropped the
  "unverified" qualifier when reporting it to Rooster as fact.
- **APPLIES DIRECTLY TO CARDS.** When I write Josh a clean declarative — "X is handled", "nothing has
  changed since Y", "the link works" — ask what scope I checked, and put THAT in the sentence. A card is
  exactly where the polish pressure is highest and where a dropped qualifier does the most damage,
  because he acts on it and repeats it to other people.

Arnn, a contact, hdclg.co, @hillsdale.edu) — email AND SMS | Donor-cultivation letters ("We strive to be different", "Teaching is my life", president's-desk essays), Imprimis-style mailings, free-course/lead-magnet offers and their SMS→email fulfillment pairs | Mass donor-cultivation / educational-marketing broadcast — no CTA, no deadline, no transaction, no personal ask.
