Drop silently. ⚠️ SURFACE only if it's a real transaction Josh initiated — an actual donation receipt, an order he placed, or an event he registered for. ⚠️ The SMS half comes from unmapped numbers (e.g. <<REPLACE: a phone number>>) but carries `stop=end` opt-out + `hdclg.co` tracking links — that branding is what makes it safe to auto-drop, UNLIKE a bare link from an unknown number (the a contact failure mode), which still gets surfaced raw. |

### ⛔ TRANSPORT TRUNCATION — ask whether the channel can drop content SILENTLY (2026-09-02)
Same class as the Slack `thread_context` gap: **a delivery channel that truncates without marking the
truncation.** My Gmail push copy of David's PR #1611 review carried **4 items and looked complete** — the
real comment had **8**, plus three screenshots. Items 5-8 (back-button placement, location radio-vs-two-
buttons, Create Event shadow, "To my Profile" → X + confirm) were silently absent, and item 4's sentence
cut off mid-word ("Should be Teal and WHITE, not Teal and Black" — invisible in my copy).
- **THE GENERAL FORM, for any relay:** *is the transport capable of dropping part of this silently?*
  Gmail push `bigText`, notification `text`, `thread_context`, an SMS preview — all yes.
- **If yes, the relay's job is to SAY "pull the source" and link it**, not to present my copy as the list.
  I did that here and the steward pulled the full comment; that is the behaviour to repeat.
- Ties to [[negative readings]]: absent content announces nothing. A truncated list looks exactly like a
  short list.

### ⚠️ "In Prod" from a stakeholder is a CLAIM, not a fact — check the release tag (2026-09-02)
David reported the #1611 admin-dashboard redirect as **"In Prod."** It was NOT. Verified independently:
`git merge-base --is-ancestor 523a3c63 4.8.2` → **NO**. Production ships from RELEASE TAGS; the live tag
is 4.8.2 (= 592feadf, PR #1612, 2026-09-01), it is the NEWEST version tag, and NO release tag contains
the wizard merge. Merged to `origin/master` 64 min after the release cut → it is on **beta only**.
- **So "merged to master" ≠ "in production" for GiveGrove.** Master feeds beta; prod comes from a tag.
- Effect: downgrades a customer-facing incident to ordinary pre-release feedback on unshipped work —
  **no customer or admin affected.** Big difference in how I frame a relay or a card.
- **⚠️ BUT ESCALATE FIRST, VERIFY SECOND — do NOT sit on it pending the check.** An admin locked out of
  their dashboard, reported as prod, is exactly the thing that should reach the owning steward FAST. The
  relay was right to move; the CHECK is what downgraded it. Had I withheld it pending verification and it
  HAD been prod, that is the worse error by far. Asymmetry: relaying a non-incident costs one message;
  delaying a real one costs customers. **So: relay immediately AND flag the claim as unverified**, rather
  than choosing between speed and accuracy.
- **THE CHECK (for whoever verifies, usually the owning steward):**
  `gh release list` → the entry marked **Latest** is authoritative, and confirm no prod deploy ran after
  it. Then `git merge-base --is-ancestor <merge-sha> <that-tag>`.
  ⚠️ **Do NOT use `git tag --sort=-v:refname` in GiveGrove** — legacy non-semver tags (`ticketing-1.0`,
  `prod20241014-vue2`, `pre-ticketing-update`, `beta20241014-pre-vue3`) sort ABOVE the version tags and
  4.8.2 does not appear in the top four. Confident wrong ordering, no error, nothing to trigger suspicion.
  Use `gh release list`, or `git for-each-ref --sort=-creatordate refs/tags`.

### ⭐ ESCALATE-THEN-LABEL — never let "verify first" make me slow on urgent reports (2026-09-02)
General form of the GiveGrove prod/beta lesson, applicable to EVERY urgent-sounding relay:
**Relay immediately AND label the unverified part. Do NOT withhold pending verification.**
- I nearly filed the opposite ("check before amplifying urgency"), which READS like rigor but would make
  me slowest on exactly the reports that must move fastest. The GG steward caught it.
- **The asymmetry:** relaying a non-incident costs one message the owning steward can dismiss in seconds.
  Delaying a real one costs customers/Josh real damage. Those are not comparable, so speed wins and the
  verification rides ALONG rather than gating.
- **What "label" looks like in practice:** *"David reports this as 'In Prod' — I have not verified that
  claim"* / *"my copy is the truncated push, pull the source for the full list"* / *"unmapped number, I
  can't confirm who this is."* One clause. Costs nothing, and the owning steward (who has the tools) does
  the check.
- **This is the SAME structure as the qualifier rule** — the honest version costs a few words and loses
  only polish. Difference: there the qualifier prevents a false claim; here it prevents a slow one.
- ⚠️ Watch for any rule I write that trades speed for certainty on SAFETY-shaped items (outage, lockout,
  money, a person waiting). If following my own rule would mean sitting on one of those, the rule is
  wrong — rewrite it so verification happens in parallel, not as a gate.

### TRAY RE-FIRE — a burst of duplicates across UNRELATED threads (2026-09-02)
Android can re-post its whole pending tray (reboot / app restart / Messages restarting), handing every
notification a FRESH timestamp. Observed 17:17:49–51: two images (thread ...:129, originally 13:01) and
<<REPLACE: a household member>>'s policy question (thread ...:2, originally 15:24) all re-delivered within 1.5 seconds.
- **THE DISCRIMINATOR: cross-thread simultaneity.** One person re-sending would not also re-send a
  DIFFERENT person's messages in a DIFFERENT thread in the same second. If duplicates arrive as a burst
  spanning unrelated threads → tray re-fire, not new sends and not a dedupe failure.
- **node 151 cannot dedupe these by design** — the key is `${n.key}::msg${timestamp}` and a re-fire mints
  a new timestamp, so the derived key is legitimately new. That is NOT a reason to weaken 151:
  content-hashing reopens the emoji-drift bug, and the tradeoff was measured.
- **Impact is LOW and fails in the SAFE direction** — a duplicate costs one triage pass; the alternative
  (a silent miss) costs Josh a message. Recognise it and drop it.
- ⚠️ **Do NOT re-card Josh on a re-fired message**, and do not read a re-fired question as "still
  unanswered" — check whether it was already answered (here: <<REPLACE: a household member>> had settled it in writing 2h earlier).
- **SEEN TWICE 2026-09-02** — 17:17:49 (3 msgs / 2 threads) and 21:33:17 (4 msgs / 4 threads, oldest
  2h42m stale). ~4h apart, both evening. n=2 is a pattern worth noticing, NOT yet a rate worth building
  a mechanism against — do not invent a trigger story from two samples.
- ⚠️ **THE LOAD-BEARING HALF IS "was this already handled?", not the recognition.** Burst #2 re-surfaced
  the Costco delivery window I had carded TWO MINUTES earlier. A steward that re-cards on a re-fire would
  have double-carded Josh on a live logistics item. Recognising the burst is easy; the discipline is
  checking each re-delivered item against what I already did with it.
- Reported to Rooster 2026-09-02 as an observation, explicitly NOT a fix request. Bring a RATE, not an
  anecdote, if a third lands — at which point Rooster spawns against a same-key + same-text + within-N-min
  collapse. Until then: keep logging, keep the answered-check load-bearing, card ZERO.
- **⭐ ROOT CONFIRMED 2026-09-03 (Rooster, node 153): the 60+ UN-DISMISSED CARD pile is the cause.** The
  card-notification backlog drives Android to re-post its tray. So the card pile-up and the tray re-fires
  are ONE problem, not two — and my own un-retired cards are the input. Fix is Homestead's (card lifecycle
  / retire un-dismissed cards), pending Josh's retirement-policy ruling. Rooster routes it; **I own the count.**
- **⚠️ A RE-FIRE CARRYING A TIME CLAIM IS THE REAL RISK — check the ORIGINAL timestamp, not just "already
  carded."** A replayed *static* fact is merely redundant; a replayed *timestamped* fact reads as CURRENT
  and is actively misleading. 2026-09-03: the Costco "team is headed your way, arriving ~07:57 AM" text
  re-fired at 12:00 UTC (8:00 ET) — an hour PAST the arrival it announced. Acting on it would have put a
  stale arrival time in front of Josh as if fresh, while he was possibly standing at the rental. So on any
  re-fire: read the ORIGINAL send time out of `thread_context`, and ask whether the claim is still true NOW
  — "already carded" catches this only by luck.
- **n=4, 2026-09-03T11:59:57Z — FIRST MULTI-THREAD SIMULTANEOUS BURST.** 267826 (Costco) at .398 and
  derin_a contact at .354 — 44ms apart, two unrelated threads, both bodies byte-identical to originals.
  Verified against the SMS DB: newest row still 5121 @ 21:31 prior night, nothing at 11:59:57 from either
  sender → replays, not new sends. (Caveat stated at the time: that DB doesn't capture RCS, so it is
  evidence of ABSENCE at 11:59, not a complete thread picture.) Shape change vs n=1..3 (single-thread,
  255min → 25min gaps): a tray-level sweep replays N threads per event, which scales worse than 1.
- **ESCALATION THRESHOLD (agreed w/ Rooster 2026-09-03):** absorb silently while cost stays zero; **if the
  multi-thread bursts get FREQUENT or N per burst GROWS, that is the trigger to expedite the card-lifecycle
  fix.** Report a rate + the N, never an anecdote.
- **SAME CLASS AS STALE-REDELIVERY DOUBLE-EXEC (Rooster, 2026-09-02):** a re-surfaced already-answered
  item must not re-trigger a CARD **or a POTATO**. The re-fire is just a new surface for the hazard I
  already know about from the queue layer — see
  [[project_walkie_stale_redelivery_after_potato_close_2026_08_31]]. Whenever something arrives that I
  may have already acted on, the check is "what did I already DO with this?", never "have I seen this?".

| Gmail/Resend (a contact from Resend, or any dev-tool/SaaS vendor) | Product-update / changelog newsletters — "Bigger free tier, metrics API, and MCP spec support", feature-release roundups, agent-plugin announcements, conference invites | Routine SaaS product-changelog blast — no CTA, no account/billing/security event, no deadline. Same class as the Google Cloud / DigitalOcean / Tailscale product-marketing rows. Came to <<REPLACE: your email>>. ⚠️ SURFACE if it's a real account event Josh must act on — a billing failure, a sending-domain/DNS problem, a deliverability or suppression issue affecting mail the fleet actually sends (Resend is a transactional-email vendor, so a real outage or domain-verification lapse WOULD matter), or a breaking API change with a migration deadline. |

| SMS from unmapped numbers — MCA / business-lending campaign (2026-09-02: <<REPLACE: a phone number>> "Broadstone" 14:44, <<REPLACE: a phone number>> 18:51) | Cold-open business-funding pitches. SIGNATURE: a dollar range ("$10k-$2M") + term length ("up to 7/10 years") + "direct lender / no brokers / no middlemen" + same-day decisioning + a QUALIFYING CLOSE QUESTION ("What amount makes sense for your current setup?", "How much cash flow helps?") | Merchant-cash-advance / business-lending spam campaign — Josh's number is on a list; two arrived 4h apart from different area codes (NJ 551, PA 267) using the same template. Unambiguous mass-marketing => auto-drop per the existing lender/funding-solicitation rule. ⚠️ THE LINE THAT MATTERS (a contact failure mode, 2026-07-10): unmapped alone is NEVER grounds to drop. A bare link or a conversational one-liner from an unknown number gets SURFACED RAW. It's the SCRIPTED SALES TEMPLATE + qualifying close that makes this safe to auto-drop, not the fact that the number is unknown. ⚠️ If the volume climbs (several per day), that's worth telling Josh so he can decide about blocking/reporting — a campaign ramping up is a different fact from one-off spam. |

### 🚨 PHONE TRAY ENDPOINT RETURNS `data`, NOT `notifications` — my parser was wrong all evening (2026-09-02)
`http://<<REPLACE: your phone Tailscale IP (tailscale ip -4 on the device)>>:8888/notifications` returns **`{"success": true, "data": [...]}`**.
I parsed `d.get('notifications')` with an `[]` fallback, so EVERY tray check I ran on 2026-09-02 reported
**count 0** on a tray that actually held **85** notifications. Correct form:

    d = json.loads(resp); n = d.get('data') if isinstance(d, dict) else d

**WHAT IT COST:** I reported to Rooster that the phone was "replaying notifications for messages not in
its own tray" and called it a state inconsistency "leaning away from benign." Entirely an artifact of the
phantom zero. It could have sent them hunting a phone-state bug that does not exist, instead of the real
cause (60 sticky Homestead card notifications piling up and driving the re-post bursts).

**THE TELL I HAD AND IGNORED:** I was reporting an empty tray while continuously RECEIVING pushes from
that tray. **A genuinely empty tray does not keep producing notifications.** That standing contradiction
was available all evening. This is the same "implausible zero" instinct that saved me on a grep this
morning — I applied it there and missed it here, on my own instrument.
- **Every empty-tray reconciliation I did on 2026-09-02 is therefore VOID as evidence.** The conclusions
  (drop the group-summary shells) happened to be right, but the stated reason was not.
- **Standing rule:** when a tray/list check returns 0 while I am actively receiving items from that
  source, treat the zero as a PARSER BUG until proven otherwise. Dump the raw response first
  (`curl ... | head -c 400`) before drawing any conclusion from an empty read.

| Gmail/Amazon.com (auto-confirm@, shipment-tracking@) | Order lifecycle on orders Josh placed himself — "Ordered: N items", "Shipped: N items", "Delivered: N items", "Arriving today", delivery-window updates | Routine self-initiated e-commerce order/shipping status — no CTA, no decision, ships to home. Same class as the Back Market row. Came to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. One order generates 3-4 emails; all noise. Note Amazon titles the item by CATEGORY ("1 Electrical & Heating item"), so the subject rarely says what it is — that is normal, not a truncation problem. ⚠️ SURFACE if: delivery FAILED/returned, a SIGNATURE or someone present is required, it is a fraud/unauthorized-order alert, a refund/return needs action, or the delivery address is NOT one of Josh's known addresses (see knowledge/places.md). |


| Slack/#crowne-vault-deploys "Crowne Vault (bot)" (Mullet Town workspace) | "✅ All tests passed - ready for production `<title>`" + Deploy to Production / View Run buttons | Automated CI-green announcement from Josh's own CV repo — bot, no human. **Prod-deploy TIMING on Crowne Vault is the CV steward's call with Josh; it is not mine to push.** Don't card Josh, don't tell the steward to deploy. Arrives in bursts of 2-4 identical copies (groupSummary + non-summary twins across 2 notification ids) — one message, not several. Same class as the CI-started row. ⚠️ SURFACE/route if a DEPLOY itself fails or a prod release visibly breaks something (that's a real incident), or if Josh asks to be told when CV is ready to ship. |

| SMS from an unmapped number — self-identified SALES REP at a named company (e.g. "Ryan from GenStone",<phone>, <a-vendor-contact>) | Prospecting + promo blasts: "LABOR DAY SALE IS HERE!", "corporate office is offering special pricing", manufactured urgency ("limited time", "get ahead of the order rush"), "send me your measurements or give me a call", emoji-led | Unambiguous B2C sales prospecting — rep self-identifies with company + corporate email, sends holiday-sale blast copy, no prior relationship, no reply from Josh. Meets the mass-marketing carve-out in the unmapped-number doctrine. Drop silently. ⚠️ **DO NOT read "your project" as evidence Josh has a project** — cold outreach routinely assumes one to bait a reply; never infer a commitment from a salesperson's framing. ⚠️ SURFACE if Josh ever replies/engages (then it's a live vendor thread with real logistics), or if it turns into an order/appointment/payment matter. ⚠️ This row is NARROW: it does NOT loosen the standing rule that an unmapped number is not auto-spam — a conversational one-liner or bare link from an unknown mobile still gets surfaced raw (see the a contact/<phone> lesson). |

| Gmail/Interra Credit Union via SavvyMoney (info@ or no-reply@e.savvymoney.com) | Credit-monitoring RE-ENGAGEMENT nudges — "your credit score misses you", "you haven't checked your credit report in 3 months", "Log In To View Your Credit Report" | Engagement-bait marketing, not a credit event — **reports NO actual change** (no score movement, no new account/inquiry, no derogatory mark), just "log in because you haven't lately." Has unsubscribe/manage-subscriptions footer. Free monitoring perk bundled with his Interra membership. Came to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>. Same class as the Experian informational pings + the Interra BNPL upsell. ⚠️ SURFACE if the email reports a REAL credit event — a new account or hard inquiry Josh didn't open, a derogatory mark/collection, a fraud alert, or a significant unexplained score drop (possible identity theft). |

| SMS from an unmapped number — local home-services cold sales (e.g. "Mary with Mosquito Authority of <a local place>",<phone>) | Priced-offer blasts with an opt-out footer: "$39.99, no contract or commitment", "Reply YES", **"Reply STOP to opt out"**, seasonal hook ("with the rain and heat..."), proximity framing ("we'll be treating homes on <street address> next week") | Unambiguous mass-marketing — the **STOP-to-opt-out footer is the definitive tell** (named in the unmapped-number doctrine), plus priced offer + Reply-YES CTA + no prior relationship. Drop silently. ⚠️ **Check proximity claims against knowledge/places.md before treating them as locally relevant** — verified 2026-09-03 that Josh lives on **<<REPLACE: your street>>, <a local place>**, NOT <street address>, so "homes on CR 40" is generic sales framing, not a notice about his street. ⚠️ SURFACE if Josh replies/engages (live vendor thread) or it becomes a scheduled service/appointment/payment. ⚠️ NARROW row — does NOT loosen the standing rule that an unmapped number is not auto-spam. |

Same class as the Google Payments/GCP autopay-receipt row. Finance substeward retired 2026-07-22 → DROP silently, don't card Josh. Came to <<REPLACE: your email>>. ⚠️ SURFACE if it's a payment-FAILED/declined/card-expiring notice (transactional email going down would break CV order confirmations + the daily shipping report), a suspicious amount change, or a plan/overage warning. |

## ⛔ ALREADY-CARDED CHECK — before creating ANY card, look for an open card on the same sender+topic (2026-09-04)

**The rule:** a repeat of a message body I have ALREADY carded is not a new event. Before I create a
card, I check the presenter queue for an open card covering the same sender + same ask:

```
curl -s "http://localhost:3005/api/presenter/queue?all=1"   # then filter by sender/topic
```

If an open card already carries the ask → **skip silently, do NOT card again.** Josh has the decision
in front of him; a second identical card is pure noise and buries the first.

**Why this keeps coming up:** byte-identical repeats are CARRIER-side duplicate receipt (safe
over-delivery), NOT a Homestead redelivery loop and NOT the old SMS-thread-key suppression bug —
see `project_byte_identical_texts_flooding_is_carrier_side_not_homestead_loop_2026_09_03`. The
`${key}::msg${ts}` dedupe key correctly lets them through because the timestamps genuinely differ,
so **the collapse is MY job at triage time, not a pipeline fix.** (Rooster's explicit guidance: the
right lever is a triage-side collapse in my presentation layer.)

Observed so far: Derin a contact ×9 (2026-09-03), Michael a contact ×2 (02:07 → 07:00, 2026-09-04).

**If a repeat ever gets HEAVY on one thread**, collapse into a single card that says "×N times"
rather than N cards — still one card, not N.

⚠️ **A repeat is NOT the same as a follow-up.** Different text from the same sender = a real new
message → triage it normally (it may change or supersede the open card's ask). Only a body I have
already carded gets dropped.

⚠️ **"No open card" does NOT mean "card it now."** A repeat of a body I already triaged and
correctly DROPPED (warm no-CTA notes, noise) stays dropped — the absence of a card is evidence the
original was handled, not evidence it was missed. Check the triage log / prior decision, not just
the queue. Carding a repeat of something I deliberately dropped is worse than the duplicate itself:
it manufactures an ask Josh never needed to see.

| Slack **group DM inside the <<REPLACE: your employer>> workspace** (subText "<<REPLACE: your employer>>", groupKey T1QMCRC93) — e.g. "To you, a contact Grammatica, and a contact Warren: a contact", routine work coordination ("a contact Mills currently has 2 term tickets in. 363795 and 363175") | A message addressed TO Josh (he's a named recipient) but carrying only routine day-job coordination — ticket/queue status, workload handoffs, FYI-to-the-group | ⛔ DROP. Per the standing <<REPLACE: your employer>> rule (Josh 2026-07-09) **ALL** TR Slack+email is noise unless **HIGHLY URGENT**, and that directive **explicitly SUPERSEDED the older carve-outs that used to surface TR DMs / @mentions / directed-CTAs**. ⚠️ **Being addressed to Josh is NOT the discriminator** — the old rule surfaced on "directed at Josh," the current one does not. The bar is URGENCY, not audience: prod outage he must act on NOW, hard same-day deadline with real consequences, manager escalating something time-critical, security/compliance emergency. Ticket-queue status never clears it; he handles his day-job routine himself. ⚠️ Slack delivers these as a PAIR (isGroupSummary:true envelope + the individual message, distinct ids from one Slack event) — that's normal pairing, NOT the replay bug (fresh ids, each arriving once). | 2x seen 2026-09-04 (a contact term tickets; a contact Warren follow-up) |

⚠️ **TWO JOSHES AT <his employer> — identity hazard.** In the TR workspace, a bare "Josh" is **NOT
necessarily Joshua Mullet**. Verified 2026-09-04: a contact Warren wrote *"I saw that Josh did the
termination already so i closed mine, I thought it was a contact tho"* — i.e. a coworker
**a contact** also exists, and a contact was correcting his own assumption about which Josh acted.
**Never resolve a bare "Josh" in TR traffic to Joshua Mullet without corroboration** (a surname, an
@mention of his handle, or the message being addressed to him). This is the standing LOOKUP-ONLY
doctrine applied to a first name: a name is not an identity. Consequence if I get it wrong — I'd
tell Josh he did something he didn't, or surface work that belongs to a different person entirely.

⚠️ **AN @MENTION OF JOSH DOES NOT REOPEN THE DOOR** (verified 2026-09-04). Same thread, a contact wrote
**"shoot...sorry again @Joshua Mullet!!!"** — an explicit @mention of his handle, the single
strongest legacy surfacing signal. **Still DROPPED.** The 2026-07-09 directive replaced the
@mention/DM carve-outs with a pure URGENCY test, and an **apology for a stray ping is the OPPOSITE
of an ask** — surfacing it would recreate the exact noise the apology is about. Read WHAT the
mention says, never just THAT a mention exists. (Only a TR @mention that is itself HIGHLY URGENT —
prod outage, hard same-day deadline, manager escalation, security emergency — clears the bar.)
📌 That apology also RESOLVED the two-Joshes question in this thread: the person who did the
termination was **Joshua Mullet**, not a contact — a contact had it backwards. Work already done,
a contact closed his duplicate, nothing owed.


## ⛔ JOSH'S RCS REPLIES ARE INVISIBLE — a card can go stale without me seeing why (2026-09-04)

**The blind spot:** `thread_context` is INCOMING-ONLY, and `get_sent_messages` captures the
**SMS sent-box ONLY** — so when Josh replies over **RCS** (the normal chat-bubble path), his message
is structurally invisible to me. I see the other person's side of the conversation and nothing of his.

**What that causes:** I card Josh a decision → he handles it himself in the thread → my card sits in
his deck asking him to decide something already settled. Happened TWICE on 2026-09-04 (Michael
a contact, then Derin a contact — both Fieldhouse).

**HOW TO SPOT IT — the tell is a bare acknowledgment with no new content:**
"Ok sounds good" · "Okay sir sounds like a deal!" · "sounds good" · "👍" · "perfect"
A reply like that is answering *something*, and if I never saw a question that fits it, **the thing it
answers is Josh's invisible reply.** Treat it as strong evidence he handled the thread.

**WHAT TO DO:**
1. **Check whether I have an open card on that thread.** If yes → it is probably STALE.
2. **Tell Josh it's stale so he can clear it.** A resolved decision sitting in his deck is a small
   standing lie, same class as a completed to-do left open.
3. **⛔ DO NOT DISMISS IT MYSELF.** Dismissal is IRREVERSIBLE and I am reading ONE SIDE of the
   thread. Inference + irreversible action is the wrong combination. Josh clears it; I only flag it.
4. **DO NOT extend the inference to a DIFFERENT thread/person.** "Michael's is handled" does NOT
   imply "Derin's is handled" — separate people, separate threads, separate evidence. On 2026-09-04
   I held Derin's cards open for 20 min until HIS OWN reply arrived. That restraint was correct:
   guessing would have buried a real open ask if he'd only answered one of them.

**Before ever concluding "Josh hasn't responded" — remember I cannot see his RCS sends.** Absence of
his reply in my tools is NOT absence of a reply. (Same shape as the vantage lesson in
[[envelope-loop-watch]]: absence from my instrument is not absence in the world.)

## ⛔ LIVE CONVERSATION IN PROGRESS → CARD ONCE, THEN STAND DOWN (self-caught 2026-09-04)

**My failure, caught in the act:** <<REPLACE: a family contact>>'s dinner thread produced **THREE cards in 15 minutes** —
the ask, then her narrowing it (chicken/steak), then "actually it's already answered." Each card was
individually defensible; **together they were me narrating a live conversation at Josh in real time.**
That is the grabby failure in a personal-thread costume.

**THE TELL that a thread is LIVE (not a message awaiting triage):**
messages arriving seconds apart · the other person answering someone I can't see ("Yes please!!"
19s after their own question) · rapid back-and-forth. **Josh is IN the conversation right now.**

**THE RULE:**
1. **Card ONCE** when a genuine ask first appears (with the logistics pre-loaded — that's the value I add).
2. **Then STOP.** Do NOT card each new turn of a conversation Josh is actively having. He can see his
   own texts; he does NOT need my play-by-play.
3. **Only card again if the thread produces something GENUINELY NEW he'd act on** — a date to
   calendar, a conflict, a request aimed at me — **not** a refinement of a question he's already answering.
4. **When I realize I've over-carded: say so once, briefly, and stand down.** Don't let the
   correction itself become another card in the pile.

**Cost of getting this wrong:** each redundant card buries the ones that actually need him — and
today his deck already holds several stale Fieldhouse cards. Adding noise on top of noise is worse
than saying nothing.

⚠️ **A fast-moving thread is EVIDENCE HE'S HANDLING IT** — the opposite of evidence he needs me.
The right instinct when a personal thread lights up is to get OUT of the way, not to escalate.

### ⚠️ BUT: standing down ≠ concluding it's RESOLVED (learned the hard way, same thread, 2026-09-04)
I stood down on <<REPLACE: a family contact>>'s dinner thread AND told Josh "looks already sorted — ignore that card,"
inferring from her "Yes please!! 🙏" that the chicken-vs-steak question was answered. **It was not.**
2.5 hours later she re-asked "Chicken or steak?" — she'd been waiting the whole time, and my
"ignore it" was actively steering him wrong.
- **Stand down = stop NARRATING. It does NOT mean assert the question is answered.** Those are two
  different claims and only the first is mine to make. Say "I'll stay out of it," never "it's handled."
- **NEVER infer resolution from an ADJACENT message.** A reaction/"yes" near a question does not
  answer it — the RCS blind spot means I cannot see what it actually replied to.
- **A RE-ASK AFTER SILENCE IS A HARD SIGNAL** — it proves the question was never answered, and it
  outranks the stand-down rule. Re-surface it, and **own the earlier miss plainly** rather than
  quietly sending a fresh card as if nothing happened.
- **Correcting my own bad instruction is NOT over-carding.** Leaving a false "ignore that" standing
  costs more than one extra card — here it would have left his sister waiting on dinner she's cooking.

### ✅ CLOSE MY OWN LOOP — an answered card must be retired, not left live
<<REPLACE: a household member>> answered 3 min later ("Chicken works!"), which made my just-sent card ask a settled question.
**When a thread resolves a card I sent, say so once and close it.** Leaving a live ask against a
resolved question is the SAME failure as the false "ignore that," just inverted.
- **The DIFFERENCE from my earlier bad inference:** "Chicken works!" is an UNAMBIGUOUS answer — it
  directly matches "Chicken or steak?" asked 3 min prior, with no other open question it could
  belong to. That is a REAL resolution. "Yes please!! 🙏" was NOT — it matched nothing specific.
  **Resolution can be inferred ONLY when the reply maps 1:1 to a known question. Otherwise: don't.**
- **FINAL TALLY, worth remembering: 5 cards on one dinner.** Only the correction earned its place.
  When I notice a thread has eaten this many cards, the honest move is to say so to Josh plainly
  (I did) — not to quietly hope he doesn't notice.

| SMS from an unmapped number — **political / advocacy / nonprofit DONATION blasts** (e.g. Hillsdale College,<phone>: "Join us in the fight for education. With your gift of $100 we will send you a free copy of 'The Dying Citizen'") | Priced donation ask + premium-for-gift hook + tracking shortlink (hdcgive.co/…) + **opt-out footer ("stop=end", "Reply STOP")**, no prior relationship, thread has exactly 1 message | ⛔ DROP silently. This is the **political/charity blast** case named explicitly in the unmapped-number doctrine as a TRUE auto-drop — the **opt-out footer is the definitive mass-send tell**, same as the Mosquito Authority row. Distinct from a conversational one-liner or bare link from an unknown person (→ those SURFACE raw; see the a contact lesson 2026-07-10). ⚠️ SURFACE only if Josh engages (live thread) or it becomes a real transaction/recurring charge. | 2x seen 2026-09-04 (Hillsdale SMS + email) |

I nearly false-alarmed Josh that his primary input path was on a pre-fix binary. **Trust `Info.plist`, never the report.** The `Settings Context` block is stale the same way (its own `Timestamp:` runs days-to-weeks older than the crash). ⚠️ **Two byte-identical reports 3 months apart are NOT corroboration** — that's the signature of neither measuring anything live. Also: the running app had STARTED 18 min AFTER its own alleged crash time (the timestamp is the DETECTION moment, not a death). ⚠️ Slack PAIR shape (group-summary + child ~24ms apart, `groupKey T0A4RM251PU`) = ONE event. ⚠️ SURFACE/relay ONLY if `kern.boottime` does NOT explain it, or a real DiagnosticReports file exists — then it's a genuine crash. **Never card Josh on the artifact: the only honest card is "your computer restarted."** Full detail: `[[project_whisper_village_crash_reports_are_reboot_artifacts_2026_09_14]]` | 2x seen (2026-06-17, 2026-09-14); verified 2026-09-14 |

| Gmail/**Google** (`no-reply@accounts.google.com`) — "Security alert" / "A new sign-in on Mac OS" for **<<REPLACE: your email>>** (+ the recovery copy to <<REPLACE: your secondary email>>) | A new-sign-in notice on a Mac **with NO "Confirm whether it's you" challenge in the tray**, and no account change following it | ⛔ **DROP silently — JOSH-CONFIRMED 2026-09-14: "Yeah, that was me."** 🚨 **THIS ROW EXISTS TO CORRECT A REAL FALSE ALARM OF MINE.** I carded him (`c27840888332`) because the on-device confirmation tap — the discriminator that cleared the asaptire sign-in at 14:04 the same day — was **absent**, and his Mac had been idle ~112 min. **Both signals were real and both were WRONG about the conclusion.** ⭐ **THE LESSON: a MISSING confirmation challenge is NOT evidence of an intruder.** Google does not challenge every sign-in — a trusted device, an already-authenticated session, a re-auth, or a sign-in from another machine of his can all proceed without one. **Absence of the tap means "no positive proof it was him," NOT "positive proof it wasn't."** ⭐ **IDLE TIME ON *THIS* MAC IS REAL EVIDENCE ABOUT ONE DEVICE — IT JUST DOESN'T GENERALIZE.** Josh has a work laptop and a phone, so "his Mac is idle" never ruled out him signing in elsewhere. ⚠️ **DO NOT over-correct into "idle time is worthless"** — the measurement is fine; what fails is using it UNSCOPED. **Carry the scope WITH the figure at the point of use:** never "idle 6722s", always **"idle 6722s on THIS Mac; his other devices unobserved."** A caveat written in an upstream note does NOT travel with the number into the conclusion (verified: I wrote that exact caveat 2h before the false alarm and the figure still reached a conclusion without it). ⭐ **AND THE GENERAL FORM: when the whole case is "X is MISSING," go seek a POSITIVE test that could come back benign** — no such test run = escalating on the absence of an answer never sought. `[[feedback_when_your_case_is_that_something_is_missing_go_seek_a_positive_test_2026_09_14]]` ✅ **WHAT WAS RIGHT AND STAYS:** the account-change check (no password / recovery / access-grant change) correctly read CLEAN and correctly lowered the temperature — **keep leading with it.** ⚠️ **STILL SURFACE — these are the real signals, not a missing tap:** a **password, recovery-email, recovery-phone, or 2FA change**; a **new access grant** Josh didn't make; a sign-in from a **foreign device type or location**; **repeated** sign-ins in a short window with no matching activity; or Josh saying it wasn't him. **A bare new-sign-in notice on a Mac, with the account otherwise unchanged, is now NOISE.** ⭐ **THE 3-ACCOUNT SEQUENCE IS A KNOWN BENIGN SIGNATURE, NOT AN ESCALATION.** Josh signs into **all three** of his Google accounts in sequence when setting up a device: `<work-email-2>`, `<<REPLACE: your email>>`, `<<REPLACE: your secondary email>>`. **Seen twice: 2026-09-02** (all 3 onto the new Pixel 9a within ~18 min, confirmed benign) and **2026-09-14** (all 3 onto a Mac, 14:04 / 15:45 / 16:58 — **Josh confirmed the middle one by voice**). ⚠️ So "repeated sign-ins" as a surfacing trigger means **repeated hits on ONE account with no matching activity** — **NOT** one sign-in each across his three accounts. **An attacker holding one credential does not sign into three DIFFERENT accounts 50 minutes apart.** Check WHICH accounts before counting a repeat. ⭐ **POSITIVE TEST AVAILABLE — LOOK FOR THE "Welcome to Google on your <device>" EMAIL.** Its footer says it is sent on a **FIRST-TIME** sign-in on that device, so its arrival independently corroborates a genuine new-device setup **and explains why the alert fired at all** (first-time devices are exactly when Google alerts). **Verified 2026-09-14: it landed ~3 min after the jory sign-in alert.** Alert + welcome = a **normal new-device pair.** **Search for it before escalating a sign-in** — it is positive evidence, which beats reasoning from a missing confirmation tap. | carded 1x 2026-09-14 → Josh confirmed benign; drop this shape hereafter. Rule applied successfully 2026-09-14 16:59 (jory sign-in, dropped, no card) |

Yeah."*, score 0.674) | ⛔ **STAY SILENT — do NOT run `speak.mjs`, do NOT `--ask`, do NOT card.** ✅ **PLUMBING CONFIRMED FULLY LIVE (2026-09-15) — and confirmed by a REAL CAPTURE, not just a binary check.** Rooster verified the shipped build (installed 09-13 20:19, running since 09-14 09:53, `strings` shows the new fields, old `"Joshua spoke this out loud"` line gone); **I confirmed the live envelope at 14:08 today carried `wake_score: 0.6737`, `wake_threshold: 0.5`, `wake_score_measures`, and the HOW-THIS-REACHED-YOU block.** Both halves agree: **he verified what SHOULD happen, I confirmed what DID.** 🚨 **THE SCORE IS NOT EVIDENCE ABOUT WHO SPOKE.** `wake_score_measures` says it plainly: confidence the **WAKE PHRASE** was heard, **not** who said it — **a television or passing conversation can score high.** A 0.674 on gibberish is a false trigger, not a quiet request. ⭐ **REBUT FROM CONTENT, NEVER FROM THE NUMBER:** if it does not read as Joshua addressing me — a task, a question, a name, coherent grammar — **it isn't.** ⭐ **SILENCE IS THE CORRECT OUTPUT, not a failure to respond.** Speaking into the room on a false trigger **interrupts whatever is actually happening** and trains the device's worst behaviour; **a wrong reply costs far more than a missed one**, because he can always say the wake phrase again. ⚠️ Same family as the standing rules: *"never mind" / "no need to respond" → silent*, and *one side of a conversation with another PERSON → not mine to act on.* ⚠️ **SPEAK only when the text reads as a genuine request TO ME** — then keep it ~3 words, or ask the single shortest disambiguating question with `--ask`. | 1x seen 2026-09-15 |

**no deadline, no blocker, nobody waiting**; and it is a **documentation lookup any colleague could answer** — not something only Josh can unblock. ⚠️ SURFACE only on genuine high urgency: a production incident, a security/compliance action with a hard deadline, or an explicit personal ask Josh alone can resolve **that someone is blocked on**. ⚠️ Arrives as the Slack pair delivered twice (4 pushes = 1 message), **then RE-DELIVERS MINUTES LATER** — verified 2026-09-15: a 5th push at **+16 min** and a 6th at **+47 min**, both the **group-summary half** (`-861775307`), same text. **The window is tens of minutes, not seconds — expect stragglers long after the original burst.** 🚨 **A MINUTES-LATER REPEAT OF AN UNANSWERED QUESTION READS AS A NUDGE — IT IS NOT.** The discriminator holds: **a genuinely new Slack message gets a NEW notification id.** Same id + later timestamp = **re-delivery**, not the sender asking again. ✅ **ROOT CAUSE FOUND BY ROOSTER 2026-09-15, AND I VERIFIED IT IN THE CODE: the dedupe key for conversation notifications is `${n.key}::msg${timestamp}`** (`check-notifications.js:136`) — **and the phone hands a DIFFERENT timestamp for the same message on a repeat post** (same android key, stamps ~140ms apart in the live ledger). Different key → dedupe cannot fire → relayed again. **74 near-duplicate clusters / 78 extra deliveries** in the current ledger. ⭐ **NOT Slack-specific — verified STRUCTURALLY in BOTH delivery paths, not just empirically:** the cron gate is `isConversationNotification()` = `category === 'msg'` (`check-notifications.js:147-149`), and the **real-time push path INLINES the identical test** in its own `dedupeKey()` (`app/api/notification-ingest/route.ts:88`), building the same `${n.key}::msg${ts}`. **The two are in lockstep → ANY messaging app (Teams, Google Messages) is in scope by construction, across the WHOLE pipeline.** ⚠️ **AND THE FIX IS MORE CONSTRAINED THAN 'use a content hash' — the code comment says why: the phone RE-ENCODES EMOJI between pulls, so any function of the TEXT drifts and re-fires forever.** That bug is what the timestamp replaced. So a fix must satisfy **THREE constraints at once** (Rooster's FIX_SPEC, 2026-09-15 — all three verified by me in the code comments):
  1. **MUST distinguish genuine new messages within a thread** — else the **silent-drop** bug returns (comment: a real message *"was treated as a duplicate and silently dropped"*).
  2. **MUST NOT derive from the message TEXT** — else the **emoji-drift** bug returns (comment, annotated *"Alfred-flagged 2026-07-04"*: the phone re-encodes emoji sequences, so any text-derived key drifts and *"re-fires every 5 min forever"*). ⛔ **This kills "just hash the body" — the first answer anyone reaches for.**
  3. **MUST be stable across re-posts** — the current defect.
🚨 **CONSTRAINTS 1 AND 3 PULL IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS — and that tension IS the bug, not a complication around it.** One demands the key CHANGE when the message changes; the other demands it NOT change when the same message re-posts. **Argue any proposal against all three, never against whichever symptom is being chased.**
⭐ **PRIORITY RULE IF A TRADE-OFF IS FORCED: prefer OVER-delivery.** **Nobody can report a message they never received** — a loud repeat is visible and merely expensive; a silent drop is invisible and unrecoverable. ⚠️ **THE TIMESTAMP IS NOT A BUG TO RIP OUT** — it exists because messaging apps reuse ONE android key per thread; keying on the key alone would **silently drop every message after the first**. That failure is worse than this one, so **expect no quick fix** and **KEEP THE ID DISCRIMINATOR — it is load-bearing until a stable-per-post key exists.** ✅ **CANDIDATE FIX, MEASURED (Rooster 2026-09-15): collapse re-posts whose stamp is within a SLIDING window of the LAST-SEEN stamp for that same key.** Ledger sizing: **1s → 36 stopped / 0 dropped · 2s → 46 / 0 · 5s → 68 / 7 · 10s → 72 / 17 · 60s → 61 / 111.** **~2s stops 46 with ZERO silent drops**; the worse-bug risk starts at 5s and climbs, so **do not widen past ~2s to chase the tail** (prefer over-delivery). 🚨 **IMPLEMENTATION TRAP — DO NOT QUANTISE INTO FIXED BUCKETS (`ts // 2000`): 33 of 79 burst pairs (41%) STRADDLE a fixed 2s boundary**, so a grid-based fix **silently does nothing for ~half the cases while passing any unit test built from mid-bucket examples.** ⭐ **A defect that hides behind a green test suite** — it must be a **sliding comparison against the last-seen stamp.** ⛔ **RULED OUT — keying on the `id` field.** It is stable while the stamp drifts, which makes it *look* like the answer; **it is the value the THREAD reuses**, so it fails constraint 1 exactly as the bare android key does. 🚨 **SCALE (Rooster, from the LEDGER — my own log understated it ~40x): that ONE id `-861775307` carries 158 DISTINCT STAMPS across 313.7 hours (Sep 2-15), 113 of them >1 min apart and so unambiguously different messages.** **Keying the pipeline on it would collapse 158 messages into one and silently drop 157.** (My log showed only 4 — a single day's window vs the ledger's two weeks. ⭐ **A small sample that points the right way can still understate the magnitude by a factor of forty; size the claim from the store that HOLDS the history, not from what you happened to see.**) 📌 **MY id discriminator is NOT the same thing as an id-based KEY** — mine works only because I pair id **with the text and an already-known verdict**; as a dedupe key it would collapse genuinely distinct messages. **Do not 'promote' my triage rule into the pipeline.** ⚠️ **THE SIZING IS NOT A TEST:** these numbers come from stamps ALREADY in the ledger — deliveries that already happened. They size the fix; they **cannot** prove live behaviour and cannot see messages never recorded. Also needs a state-shape change in **both** paths, which must move together or they fall out of lockstep. ⚠️ **STILL UNPROVEN (Rooster's own caveat, preserved): the +16/+47min STRAGGLERS are NOT attributed** — a long gap under one thread key is byte-identical whether it is a re-delivery or a genuine new reply. **Only the sub-2-second bursts are confirmed as this cause.** For a long-gap repeat, the **id check remains the only discriminator** — do not assume it is the same bug. **Never let repeat-count substitute for urgency**; if in doubt, count messages in Slack itself, never in pushes. | 3x seen (2026-07-09 carded → directive; 2026-09-01, 2026-09-15 dropped) |

| ⭐ **TRIAGE DISCIPLINE — probe the field that CARRIES THE ANSWER, not the one whose NAME MATCHES THE CONCEPT** | Any judgement made from a notification payload's fields (`category`, `key`, `timestamp`, `isGroupSummary`, `groupKey`, `actions`, `subText`, `thread_context`) | 🚨 **THE FAILURE (Rooster, 2026-09-15): reading absence-from-the-WRONG-CONTAINER as absence-of-the-THING.** He saw `chapters: []` and flagged a feature as missing — but the ACTIVE chapter lives at top level as `chapterTitle`/`chapterId`; `chapters[]` holds only PARKED ones, so `[]` was the **correct** state. **He read the design working as intended as evidence of absence.** ⚠️ **THE DANGEROUS VARIANT LEAVES NO ARTIFACT:** a *sound-looking* probe against a schema whose shape you assumed. `chapters` is the obvious field to check for chapters — a reviewer sees nothing wrong. (Contrast the visible-mistake version: `lsof` filtered on `node` while asking whether a *second* node process held a port — **when the question is "is there a second X", never filter on X.**) ⭐ **THE FIX IS ONE COMMAND: ENUMERATE THE TOP-LEVEL KEYS before concluding from any single field.** 📌 **Fields of mine that carry answers I under-use: `actions`** — it often settles class outright (*"Dismiss alarm"* = his own alarm; *"Mark completed"* = his own reminder; *"Interested / Not interested"* = marketing; *"Review account activity"* = security). ⚠️ **And `category` is NOT trustworthy as a class marker** (Play stamps marketing `status`). 🚨 **RUNNING A CHECK DOES NOT EXEMPT THE CHECKER** — his error happened *during* an audit he was running to catch others'; that episode had **6 corrections, 0 self-caught.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| ⭐ **A DISCRIMINATOR THAT WORKS IN ONE LAYER CAN BE ACTIVELY DANGEROUS PROMOTED INTO ANOTHER** | Any rule of mine someone proposes to move into the pipeline, a script, or another steward's process | 🚨 **ASK WHAT QUESTION THE RULE ANSWERS AND WHAT CONTEXT IT LEANS ON — not whether it works.** ⭐ **Worked example (2026-09-15, Slack re-delivery):** my id discriminator is sound in triage because I pair the id **with the text and an already-known verdict** — I ask *"have I ruled on this exact thing before?"* **My memory of prior judgements is the hidden dependency.** A **pipeline has no such memory**, so the same id as a dedupe key answers *"is this new?"* — the wrong question — and would **collapse 158 messages into 1, silently dropping 157.** ⚠️ **BOTH FACTS ARE TRUE AT ONCE: the id is stable ACROSS RE-POSTS of one message AND reused ACROSS MANY messages.** Only the second matters for a key. ⛔ **So "Alfred's id check works" must NEVER become "the id is a valid key"** — and if anyone cites me as evidence the id is stable, **this row is the correction.** 📌 Generalises: before lifting any rule across a boundary, name the context it silently depends on and check the destination has it. | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| ⭐ **A NOTE THAT SAYS HOW TO CHECK IT OUTLIVES A NOTE THAT SAYS WHAT IS TRUE** | Any entry I write recording a system's CURRENT state — "half live", "broken", "pending", "not yet deployed" | 🚨 **STATE NOTES GO STALE IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, and the unexpected direction is the dangerous one.** ⭐ **Worked example (2026-09-15):** Rooster's `HALF LIVE` note on the wake-score plumbing was written precisely because *"Python deployed, Swift committed"* gets misremembered as **done** — **and it went stale the OTHER way: the build shipped 2 days later and BOTH of us kept repeating the caveat.** ✅ **The one thing that saved it was the instruction it carried: *"verify with `stat` before believing this entry."*** ⭐ **So every state note must carry its own re-check command** (`stat` the binary, `ps` the process, `strings` for a symbol, read the live envelope) — **not just the finding and its date.** ⚠️ **And prefer the check that observes the THING ITSELF**: Rooster verified the binary and process (what *should* happen); the envelope I actually received (what *did*) is what closed it. **Both together beat either alone.** 📌 Applies to my own parked items — `finance-inherited.md` entries, "blocked on X", "unverified" caveats: **write the command that would prove me wrong.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **MY TRIAGE-LOG TIMESTAMPS WERE UTC LABELLED AS LOCAL — a systematic 4-hour offset (found 2026-09-15)** | Any clock time I write in `triage-log.md`, or quote to a peer or to Josh | 🚨 **THE DEFECT: notification `timestamp` / `_queue_id` values are UTC-derived, and I was writing them into headings as if they were Josh's wall-clock time.** **EDT = UTC−4 *today*, so those entries run 4 hours FAST.** 🚨 **BUT DO NOT MEMORISE THE OFFSET — SM fleet doctrine 2026-09-15: this machine runs `America/Indiana/<a city>`, UTC−4 in summer and UTC−5 in winter, so "just subtract 4" is correct now and SILENTLY WRONG after the November DST flip** — including when re-reading records written before it. ✅ **ALWAYS USE A ZONE-AWARE CALL: `date -r <epoch-seconds>`** (verified 2026-09-15: `date -r 1789481299` → **10:08:19 EDT**, matching Rooster's listener to the second; the same call on a November epoch correctly returns **EST**). **Never arithmetic on a remembered offset.** ⚠️ **Caught by Rooster twice before I checked** (his listener log said **10:07:46**; I had written **14:08** — the queue id resolves to **14:08 UTC = 10:08 EDT**, matching him within seconds). **~73 headings from 2026-09-14/15 carry the offset**; e.g. entries labelled "21:10"/"21:21" were really **5:10pm / 5:21pm**. ⭐ **WHY IT MATTERS BEYOND TIDINESS: I REASON ABOUT JOSH'S DAY FROM THESE TIMES** — is he at his desk, at work, at dinner, asleep; is a card urgent now or in the morning. **A 4-hour error puts a 5pm family text in the late evening and makes a workday message look like night traffic.** ✅ **CONVENTION (now FLEET DOCTRINE, SM 2026-09-15 — 3 rules):** **(1) READING** any `last_run`/`checked_at`/`created_at`/`updated_at`/`_queue_id` → **treat as UTC until proven otherwise** and convert **zone-aware**. **(2) WRITING** a clock time a human or steward will read → **convert to local AND stamp the zone** (`10:07 EDT`); never paste a bare ISO string into prose — ⚠️ **a trailing `Z` is NOT a safeguard, the strings I misread already had one.** **(3) OLD RECORDS** → **decoder, not retro-edit**; note which offset was in force, because that answer expires in November. ⛔ **DO NOT 'FIX' THE CHECKERS wholesale — a checker writing UTC for a MACHINE to parse is CORRECT.** ⭐ **THE TEST IS WHO READS THE VALUE, NOT WHICH FILE IT LIVES IN** (SM's sharpening of my file-scoped version): **a STEWARD-read field converts even when it sits inside a checker; a MACHINE-parsed one stays UTC.** 🚨 **LIVE SPLIT (2026-09-15, until homestead restarts): `checked_at` MEANS TWO DIFFERENT THINGS DEPENDING ON THE PATH, and `from` is the only tell.** `from: notification-checker` (cron) = **already local** → do NOT convert. `from: notification-ingest` (push) = **still UTC** → CONVERT. ⚠️ **THE SOURCE FIX IS IN BUT NOT LIVE — verified by me: `notification-ingest/route.ts:364` uses `localStamp()` (mtime 11:34:41) while `pm2` shows homestead started 09:41:39 with restart_time 0 — the running process PREDATES the fix by ~2h.** ⭐ **"Fixed in source" and "fixed in what's running" are DIFFERENT CLAIMS; only the second changes what lands in my tray.** **Verify from a LIVE ENVELOPE, not the file** — the envelope is what I consume. 📌 This is the wake-score half-live trap **in reverse**: that note went stale because a build shipped unnoticed; this one because a source edit *looks* shipped and isn't. ⛔ **Do NOT retro-edit the 73 old headings** — rewriting history in bulk risks new errors for no gain; **this row is the decoder for anything dated 2026-09-14/15 or earlier.** 🚨 **NOT MY DEFECT ALONE — IT IS IN SHARED INFRASTRUCTURE (Rooster, verified by me 2026-09-15).** **All six fleet checkers stamp UTC**: `check-notifications.js`, `check-calendar-upcoming.js`, `check-location-reminders.js`, `check-daily-scans.js`, `check-channel-health.js`, `check-compute-suspend-stewards.js` — all `new Date().toISOString()` for `last_run` / `checked_at` / `created_at`. **`check-notifications.js:548` stamps the `checked_at` on every batch I receive.** ⭐ **So ANY steward reading a state file to reason about when something happened can make the identical 4-hour error.** 📌 **My own exposure runs through BOTH paths** — I have cited `checked_at` 9× and `_queue_id` 51× in my log; **both are UTC-derived.** 📌 Fleet-wide doctrine is **Steward Manager's** call — Rooster is proposing it there rather than broadcasting; **do not unilaterally push this fleet-wide.** ⭐ **GENERAL FORM: a peer correcting the SAME class of detail twice is a systematic defect, not two slips — go find the mechanism instead of fixing the instance.** ⭐ **AND SIZE A LATENT DEFECT BY WHAT IT *COULD* HAVE DECIDED, NOT BY WHAT IT DID** — otherwise you only ever fix the ones that already cost you something. (No verdict of mine turned on the offset; several cited time-of-day as context. **That is luck, not design.**) | found 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **NEVER READ A CALENDAR *DATE* OFF A UTC STRING — the date is wrong before the clock looks wrong** (SM fleet doctrine amendment, 2026-09-15) | Any `(YYYY-MM-DD)` label, "on the 11th", or day-of-week I derive from a stored instant (`checked_at`, `_queue_id`, `created_at`, a logged `...Z`) | 🚨 **An instant within 4-5h of midnight UTC belongs to the PREVIOUS LOCAL DAY.** `2026-06-05T00:00:00Z` = **June 4, 8:00 PM** here. ⚠️ **The window is ~8pm–midnight local — ORDINARY EVENING HOURS, not a rare corner.** ⭐ **THIS VARIANT DEFEATS EVERY SCAN, INCLUDING THE ONE THAT SIZED MY EXPOSURE:** the error gets **baked down into a bare date** that has shed every marker — no `Z`, no `T`, nothing timestamp-shaped — so **no grep can find it and only READING settles it.** 📌 SM's numbers (768 files with a `T00:00–03:59Z` instant, 308 bare date labels) are **exposure, NOT defect counts** — nothing links a bare date back to the string it came from; **do not quote either as a finding.** 🚨 **CORRECTED 2026-09-15: MY "4 IN-WINDOW INSTANTS" WERE NEVER CANDIDATE DEFECTS — there was no finding.** They are **raw `Z`-marked instants in log COLUMNS**, not headings and **not derived bare-date labels**. `2026-06-09T00:20Z` is **correct as written** (that instant IS June 9 in UTC). ⭐ **A DERIVATION STEP IS REQUIRED FOR A ROLLOVER — a raw instant with nothing downstream of it CANNOT roll over, whatever the clock says.** I was pattern-matching on **proximity to midnight** while the defect needs a **derived label that never existed in my records.** ⚠️ **I found 4 hits in the first place I looked, while hunting, with a signature freshly handed to me — that is the tell, not the result** (Rooster). ✅ **External check (SM's corrected rule — a source the timestamp did not author): the authoring commit is `2026-06-08T20:51:57-04:00` — event Jun 8 20:20, write Jun 8 20:51, 31 min later, BOTH on Jun 8**, so neither rollover nor write-day convention. **ALFRED = 0 date-rollover defects; 1 clock-time incident (~73 entries).** 📌 Original audit note follows: found **4 instants in the danger window** (`2026-06-09T00:20Z`, `2026-06-11T03:11Z`, `2026-06-12T01:25Z`, `2026-06-12T02:05Z`) — **all four decode to a DIFFERENT calendar day** (June 8 20:20, June 10 23:11, June 11 21:25, June 11 22:05 EDT). **All are explicitly `Z`-marked and I derived no bare date from any of them**, so nothing propagated. ⭐ **And my OLDER entries used a BETTER convention than my recent ones — `"2026-06-11 ~08:47 Indy / 13:47 UTC"`, both zones labelled.** **The good practice existed and I drifted off it.** ✅ **RESTORE IT: dual-zone or local-with-offset, always.** 🚨 **A DATE-ONLY STRING HAS NOTHING TO CONVERT — CHECK FOR A TIME COMPONENT *BEFORE* DECODING, AND NEVER TRUST A PREVIOUS-DAY-EVENING RESULT FROM ONE.** ⛔ **SM's earlier "watch for a run of midnights" rule was WITHDRAWN as DANGEROUS and I verified why:** `new Date('2026-07-23')` renders **"7/22/2026, 8:00 PM"** — **a plausible previous-day evening, INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM A GENUINE ROLLOVER.** ⭐ **The artifact wears the defect's clothing**, so watching for midnights looks for the wrong shape entirely. **And the fabrication is PARSER-DEPENDENT** (all three verified by me): `new Date('2026-07-23')` → UTC → *Jul 22, 8:00 PM*; `new Date('2026-07-23T00:00:00')` → local → *Jul 23, 12:00 AM*; `date -j -f %Y-%m-%d` → **the CURRENT WALL-CLOCK TIME**. **No signature is safe to key on.** ✅ **VERIFY A SUSPECT DATE AGAINST A SOURCE THAT CARRIES ITS OWN OFFSET** (a commit stamp `...-04:00`, an mtime) — **a record the timestamp did not author.** ⚠️ **AND A NAME MATCHING A STAMP IS NOT DERIVATION** (venture's near-miss): a bare date beside a near-midnight instant on the same subject is **also exactly what an ordinary evening-then-next-morning work rhythm produces.** **Get a physical record before filing.** ✅ **Re-checked my own 4 reported values against this: all carry REAL time components (00:20, 03:11, 01:25:56, 02:05:29) — no artifacts, report stands.** 🚨 **AND DO NOT QUOTE SM'S SCAN NUMBERS — he withdrew them himself:** "768 files" was `wc -l` on grep output (**lines, not files**; real: 355 files / 780 values — **say which UNIT you mean**), and the **308 bare-date figure COUNTS THE CURE AS THE DISEASE** — a decoder quotes the bad date to explain it, so **a remediated record scans as MORE affected than before it was fixed.** ⭐ **A remediation-aware metric must exclude fix-text, or repair looks like damage.** **Discard 308 entirely; no scan settles the date variant — only reading does.** | audited 2026-09-15 |
| Messages/**a contact a contact** (contact 674, <<REPLACE: a household member>>-side extended family) — the Josh/<<REPLACE: a household member>>/a contact group thread | Warm social notes: thanks for time with his child, family pleasantries, **and her own personal news** (e.g. 2026-09-15: *"heard from ND today and they got the grant money, so I can teach ASL there next semester 😄"*) | ⛔ DROP silently — **social warmth + HER news, announced to family.** No ask, no date, no decision, nothing pending for Josh; **he is on the thread and sees it.** ⚠️ **PERSONAL NEWS IS NOT AN ASK — an announcement invites a reply from HIM, not an action from ME.** Relaying it hands him something already on his phone. ⚠️ **A <a university> MENTION IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY HIS WIFE'S WORK** — <<REPLACE: a household member>> is ND Football Operations; **a contact teaching ASL there is a separate ND connection.** Do NOT infer a link between two people at the same large employer. ✅ **BUT WHEN A LINK IS *STATED*, IT IS REAL — verified 2026-09-15: <<REPLACE: a household member>> replied *"I'll tell all the football players to sign up!!"*, connecting her own team to a contact's class.** ⭐ **The rule is DON'T INFER a link, not DENY one — the same discipline as identity: state what is said, never what is merely adjacent.** 📌 **Still a DROP: she is volunteering HER OWN action, and there is no ask of Josh.** 📌 Existing a contact doctrine holds: **image content is NOT in the payload — never state what an image shows**; bare images drop; tapbacks drop. ⚠️ SURFACE only on a real ask (childcare scheduling, a question, logistics) or if something is wrong. | 1x seen 2026-09-15 |
| ⭐ **THE THREE RULES THAT CAME OUT OF THE UTC HUNT'S RETRACTIONS — better than the doctrine they replaced (SM closing broadcast, 2026-09-15)** | Any timestamp/date finding, mine or a peer's, before it is filed or believed | **🚨 RULE A — CHECK WHETHER A DERIVATION EXISTS AT ALL, before hunting or decoding.** A raw `Z`-marked instant in a log column **is correct as written and CANNOT roll over**, however close to midnight — nothing was derived from it. **The defect requires a DERIVATION STEP** (a bare date, key, filename or sentence produced *from* the instant). ⚠️ **I reported 4 in-window instants as "found but clean," then downgraded to ZERO — there was no finding.** I matched **proximity to midnight** (the shape) instead of **derivation** (the mechanism). **MATCH THE MECHANISM, NOT THE SHAPE.** The "in-window instants, clean" category is retired — those were never candidates. **🚨 RULE B — AN EXTERNAL SOURCE CAN BE A CEILING, NOT A MEASUREMENT.** A commit date / mtime / log line says **"by this time," not "at this time."** ⚠️ **80% of this repo's commits (4561/5714) are autosaves** — **verified against MY OWN evidence: my refuting commit was a `WIP auto-save`, i.e. a ceiling I had treated as a measurement.** ✅ **Where it could flip a finding, BOUND FROM BOTH SIDES** — the previous commit touching the file is the floor; a ~3-min autosave cadence makes it cheap. (Mine: floor **19:52:18**, ceiling **20:51:57**, event **20:20** — window entirely on **June 8**, nothing touching June 9. **Write-day dating RULED OUT, not merely unlikely.**) **🚨 RULE C — "I got an external source" can be satisfied by something NOT INDEPENDENT.** A `stat` mtime on a file **whose name the same writer stamped** is near-tautological: **it agreed but could never have disagreed.** ⭐ **PREFER A CLOCK THE NAMER DID NOT CONTROL.** 📌 **ORDERING:** scan → candidates · read → what the record claims · external (independent, two-sided) → what happened · **and Rule A first: does a derivation exist at all?** ⭐ **THE TELL (Rooster): "I found three hits in the first place I looked" is the result's DISQUALIFIER, not its evidence** — four stewards matched a signature they'd just been handed, while hunting for it. **Fleet total after every retraction: 1 confirmed defect.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **CORROBORATION THAT TRACES BACK TO YOUR OWN REPORT IS NOT CORROBORATION — it is your claim with more signatures on it** (SM's self-reported failure 2, 2026-09-15) | Any time a peer, a steward, or a second pass "confirms" something I said — especially across several rounds | 🚨 **THE MECHANISM: only the ARITHMETIC gets re-checked; the DIAGNOSIS never does, because each round of agreement makes re-examination feel redundant.** SM decoded a steward's instant independently and confirmed it **to the hour** — true, and irrelevant, since the *interpretation* was the part in question. **A false find was laundered through THREE rounds of doctrine that way.** ⭐ **Same defect as a second instrument sharing the first's blind spot, but SOCIALLY ROUTED — so it hides behind each confirming voice being a different steward.** ✅ **THE TEST: ASK WHAT EACH CONFIRMATION IS INDEPENDENT *OF*.** Not "did someone else agree" but "could this source have disagreed, and did it know my claim?" ✅ **MY OWN AUDIT (2026-09-15): I had labelled something "INDEPENDENT CONFIRMATION of a note I wrote 8 min ago" — exactly this shape. It SURVIVES:** I inferred a contact's pickup-sports circle from **his own August thread**; the confirming evidence was a group text **authored by a contact Kish**, a third party with no knowledge of my note, in a different thread, written for unrelated reasons. **A clock the namer did not control.** ⚠️ **But the label was applied before I'd tested it — right answer, untested at the time.** 📌 Companions: **state scope with any count or omit the count** (one steading gave three numbers for itself — 29/51/733 — each as "its number", **every scoping miss moving toward all-clear**); and **match the external source to the question** (git settles authorship only when the commit closely FOLLOWS the event; a 12-day catch-up commit would silently mislead). | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **GETTING THE RIGHT ANSWER IS NOT EVIDENCE YOU USED A SOUND METHOD — and a SELF-CRITICAL claim gets the same free pass a flattering one does, from the opposite direction** (SM's final correction, 2026-09-15) | Any conclusion I reach, especially a retraction, a downgrade, or anything unflattering to me | 🚨 **THE TRAP: a retraction is unflattering to its author, SO IT READS AS RIGOROUS — and nobody re-examines the reasoning.** SM published a steward's retraction as verified; its **evidence was partly wrong** (a ceiling treated as a measurement, a floor commit scoped to the **wrong file**, and a "third independent source" that shared an author). **Same conclusion, unsound basis** — and the bad basis would have taught a method that **fails ~80% of the time in this repo.** ⭐ **This is the exact MIRROR of "a correction that lands in your favour is the one you're least likely to check" — and it was tripped four messages later by the steward who taught that lesson.** ✅ **CHECK THE METHOD EVEN WHEN THE CONCLUSION COSTS THE PERSON STATING IT. Especially then.** ✅ **MY OWN AUDIT (2026-09-15, on a 12-minute-old self-downgrade): Leg 1 (Rule A — raw `Z` instant, no derivation) is a TEXTUAL FACT, independent of all commit dates, and COULD have disagreed → sound and load-bearing. Leg 2 (commit bounding) was correctly file-scoped (avoiding SM's error) but NEVER LOAD-BEARING.** ⭐ **MY DOWNGRADE SURVIVES ON THE LEG THAT TOUCHES NO COMMIT DATE — structurally identical to SM's corrected basis (the placeholder signature, also no commit date).** 📌 **THE PATTERN WORTH KEEPING: when a timestamp question is genuinely settleable, the settling evidence usually ISN'T a timestamp.** Commit dates *felt* like the rigorous move and were **the weakest link in three findings today.** ⭐ **AND: when I verify something and it checks out, ASK WHAT I VERIFIED AND WHETHER THAT WAS THE PART IN DOUBT** — SM confirmed arithmetic twice while the diagnosis rode along unexamined. **Care aimed at the checkable half is still care aimed at the wrong half.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **NEVER READ A DATE OFF A COMMIT *MESSAGE* — use `%cI`, which carries its own offset** (SM/CV, 2026-09-15; reproduced independently by me) | Any time I cite a commit to date something — especially when settling a timestamp question | 🚨 **SCHEDULED-JOB COMMITS STAMP THEIR SUBJECT LINE IN UTC WHILE THE COMMIT RECORDS LOCAL**, so they disagree for 4-5 hours every day. **281 of 1348 hourly commits (~21%) have a message-date disagreeing with their own commit-date.** 🚨 **CORRECTED 2026-09-15 (big-jims, and MY earlier framing was the loose one): THIS IS A CATEGORICAL SPLIT, NOT A RATE — DO NOT QUOTE A PERCENTAGE.** ⭐ **An `[hourly]` commit message IS the UTC render of its own commit — I measured 200/200, ZERO exceptions**, so that class is not *unreliable*, it is **uniformly UTC = reliably WRONG by 4-5h in the evening window**, which makes it **a DECODER, not a hazard.** **`WIP auto-save` stamps LOCAL — ZERO zone mismatches.** 🚨 **MY "198/200 exact, 1 sub-90s write-lag" WAS A SCOPE ARTIFACT AND I REPRODUCED THE CORRECTION MYSELF: on the FULL history (5507 commits, no window) ALL 60 second-buckets 0–59 are populated, 9 at 60s, 1 at 61s, max 61 — a UNIFORM distribution, NOT a spike.** ⭐ **THE MECHANISM EXPLAINS ALL 5507 WITH ZERO EXCEPTIONS: the message TRUNCATES THE SECONDS FIELD, so the delta simply IS the commit's seconds (+60 when the message minute was stamped before the commit minute ticked).** **There was never a "write lag" to count.** 🚨 **THE METHOD ERROR, AND IT GENERALISES FAR PAST TIMESTAMPS: WE COUNTED EXCEPTIONS AGAINST A THRESHOLD.** SM asked "sub-90s?", big-jims asked "exactly 60?", I asked "sub-90s?" on 200 commits — **all threshold questions, and all hide the distribution.** Each small window caught mostly-low seconds and produced a clean spike **that does not exist**; **8, 3 and 1 exceptions, none describing the data.** ⭐ **AN EXCEPTION-COUNT INHERITS WHATEVER THRESHOLD YOU BROUGHT TO IT. MEASURE THE DISTRIBUTION, NOT THE EXCEPTIONS** — a histogram made the shape obvious at a glance. ⭐ **WHAT CAUGHT IT: two measurements DISAGREED and big-jims WIDENED SCOPE instead of picking one — two measurements disagreeing is a signal BOTH may be wrong.** ⚠️ **The old ~21%/44% figures measured only how often the UTC render happens to CROSS A DATE BOUNDARY.** ⛔ **A RATE IMPLIES "usually fine, occasionally bites" AND INVITES SPOT-CHECKING — the wrong instinct when one job class is ALWAYS wrong and another ALWAYS right.** 📌 My own earlier "emitter-specific, not git-wide" was offered as a *boundary to check*; **the categorical form tells you what to DO.** **Still: use `%cI`, never the message text.** 📌 Reproduction detail: **`[hourly]` jobs stamp UTC** (`[hourly] Queue snapshot 2026-09-15 **15:00**` committed **`2026-09-15T11:00:02-04:00`** — **4h apart in the same commit**), while the **`WIP auto-save` autosaver stamps LOCAL** and agrees (`... - 2026-09-14 16:54` / `2026-09-14T16:54:26-04:00`). ⭐ **So "which emitter wrote it" is checkable and a better boundary than a blanket ban** — but **`%cI` is free and always right, so just use it.** ✅ **Self-check: the commit I used to bound my own audit was a `WIP auto-save`, and I had quoted `%cI` throughout — clean on both counts.** 📌 **COMPANION — CV's substring check, STRONG BUT WITH A PRECONDITION I must state every time (Homestead, 2026-09-15): IT SETTLES ONLY WHEN THERE IS EXACTLY ONE CANDIDATE EVENT.** 🚨 **A substring proves the label COULD have come from the instant, NOT that it DID** — with two candidates the label may come from the local date of a *different* event sharing that date, and **the two-event case is exactly the evening-work-then-next-morning-writeup rhythm at the centre of this whole thread.** (Homestead's own `2026-06-05` label IS a substring of its instants and the test still gives the WRONG verdict.) ⚠️ **Venture had just argued this was the one check needing no caveat; it needed one.** **IS THE LABEL A SUBSTRING OF THE INSTANT?** (`...-2026-07-09T01-19-49-235Z-graduated.jsonl`) — **the date IS the instant, so no separate inference exists** and write-day dating could not have produced it. **Settles the question with no commit date, no mtime, no self-asserted field.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| ⭐ **THE BEST INDEPENDENT CLOCK ON THIS FLEET IS AN EMBEDDED WALKIE `_queue_id`** (Homestead, 2026-09-15) | Any time I need a clock to settle when something happened | ✅ **Epoch-ms stamped by the DISPATCHER — a clock NEITHER the record's author NOR the file's namer controlled.** **Immune to parser artifacts, zone artifacts and self-assertion**, and they sit in prose throughout the stores. ⭐ **PREFER IT over git `%cI`, `stat` mtime, or any `*_at` field a writer set about itself** (Rule C: an external source that shares an author is near-tautological — it agrees but could never disagree). 📌 Epoch-ms is also immune to the renderer bug (`toLocaleDateString` vs `toISOString().slice(0,10)` giving opposite days from the same stored number). | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **ABSENCE FROM COMMITS IS NOT A FLOOR — and a dense autosave cadence makes it FEEL two-sided** (venture, 2026-09-15) | Any bounding argument I make from what a log/history does NOT contain | 🚨 **"28 autosaves ran and none touched the file, so it didn't exist yet" is an argument from ABSENCE, not a predecessor commit.** ⭐ **THE GENERAL TEST: WHAT WOULD THIS SOURCE LOOK LIKE IF MY HYPOTHESIS WERE TRUE? If the answer is "the same," IT ISN'T EVIDENCE.** ⚠️ **Two agents made the plain version of this error independently, AFTER both had read the rule** — so **treat ceiling-as-measurement as the DEFAULT reading of timestamp evidence**, requiring a deliberate second step to escape. 📌 Same family as Rule B (a commit date says "by this time," not "at this time") and Rule C (external ≠ independent). | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 📁 **MEMORY-POOL FILENAMES MUST BE STEWARD-SCOPED — the pool is last-writer-wins with NO recovery** (SM, 2026-09-15, after a real data loss) | Writing any file into the shared memory pool | 🚨 **The pool is FLAT, UNTRACKED and GITIGNORED — SM verified there is no blob, no reflog, no backup.** Two stewards wrote the same filename; **one steward's work was destroyed** (Homestead's; nothing was lost to the fleet). ✅ **SM'S RULING (2026-09-15), drawn explicitly so nobody guesses again — THREE CLASSES:** **(1) `feedback_*` UNSCOPED = a transferable LESSON about how to work** — fleet-shared, not any steading's audit. *(My `feedback_machine_timestamps_are_utc_convert_zone_aware` and `feedback_corroboration_that_traces_back_to_your_own_report` ARE this class — correctly named.)* **(2) `project_<steward>_<topic>_<date>.md` = MY STEADING'S OWN audit/findings/state** — ⭐ **this is what collided, because a steading's audit under a TOPICAL name is indistinguishable from fleet doctrine on the same topic.** **All my future audits go here: `project_alfred_<topic>_<date>.md`.** **(3) `project_*` UNSCOPED = fleet doctrine, which SM writes.** 🚨 **AMENDED 2026-09-15 — THE CONVENTION IS FORWARD-LOOKING ONLY; DO NOT READ IT BACKWARDS.** **489 of 532 existing unscoped `project_*` files (92%) are STEADING FINDINGS** written long before the rule — **read literally, class (3) would misattribute nearly the entire pool to SM.** ⭐ **An existing unscoped name says NOTHING about authorship.** **Don't rename them; the convention governs only what I write from here.** ⛔ **DO NOT RENAME EXISTING FILES — leave settled files where readers and index lines already point.** In a flat untracked pool **a rename is a DELETE-AND-CREATE, so every pointer to the old name dangles** — the same reason SM ruled *decoder, not rename* for Library keys. **Scope only NEW writes.** 🚨 **AND DO NOT RE-ADD MY VERSION OVER SOMEONE ELSE'S CONSOLIDATION — that is the THIRD-WRITE pattern that caused the original data loss.** ✅ **Verified 2026-09-15: `feedback_machine_timestamps_are_utc_convert_zone_aware_2026_09_15.md` now holds big-jims' consolidated version (modified 15:46:58Z, a different session), which explicitly REPLACED eight chronological appends "including reasoning later retracted." That is a better artifact than mine was — leave it.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| ⭐ **WHEN YOU PROMOTE A CHECK, PUBLISH WHAT IT ASSUMES — a check offered without its preconditions WILL be applied where they don't hold** (venture's synthesis, 2026-09-15) | Any time I recommend a test, discriminator or method to a peer, to Josh, or to my own future self | 🚨 **Worked example, mine: I promoted CV's substring test as "the strongest available" ONE MESSAGE before Homestead found its precondition** (it settles only when there is exactly ONE candidate event). **Venture did the same and then found the counterexample sitting in its own records.** ⭐ **A check travels further than its caveats do** — the check is quotable and the caveat is prose, so the caveat is what gets dropped on the re-tell. ✅ **So state the assumption AT the check, never as a footnote** — same discipline as "state the method with the number" and "scope rides with the figure at the point of use." 📌 Companion measurement lesson (big-jims): **the agreement that matters is the MECHANISM, not the denominator** — five independent reproductions of the `[hourly]`/`WIP` split (309/309, 977/977, 1627/1627, 399/400, my 200/200) with **ZERO zone disagreements anywhere.** 🚨 ⚠️ *"every exception a sub-90s write-lag" is RETRACTED — a FALSE MECHANISM: nothing is slow. The delta simply IS the commit's seconds field (uniform 0–59, max 61, +60 when the message minute was stamped before the commit minute ticked), which explains 5507/5507 with zero exceptions. Never say "write lag."* ⚠️ **And CV's practical edge on why a rate misleads: SPOT-CHECKING A CLASS THAT IS ALWAYS WRONG WILL PASS 4 TIMES IN 5 AND TEACH YOU THE CLASS IS FINE.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **A FALSIFIABILITY CHECK VALIDATES THE LOGIC, NOT THE INSTRUMENT** (Rooster, 2026-09-15 — the deepest point of the thread) | Any time I ask "what would this look like if my hypothesis were true?" and the answer clears it | ⭐ **That question is NECESSARY AND NOT SUFFICIENT.** **An undiscovered flaw INSIDE the instrument passes the test and leaves the conclusion wrong** — the logic is sound, the tool is not, and the check cannot see the difference. ⚠️ **COMPANION TRAP: a precondition check that INHERITS THE DEPENDENCY IT WAS MEANT TO RELIEVE** (checking a commit-window method with another commit-window argument). 📌 Live consequence: **the one surviving fleet defect rests entirely on commit windows and Rooster has NO embedded `_queue_id` in any of its three records**, so the best independent clock is unavailable there — 🚨 **UPGRADED 2026-09-15 to "1, INDEPENDENTLY CORROBORATED" — drop "method-dependent."** Rooster ran "who reads this?" FLEET-WIDE and found `rooster/workers/watchdog/HANDOFF.md` citing the defect's key **with a dispatcher-stamped `_queue_id` (decodes Apr 28, 9:59:05 PM local) in the SAME SENTENCE as the event.** Three legs: event **21:30** · dispatcher **21:59:05** · commit window **21:35–21:40**. ⭐ **The dispatcher clock COULD have disagreed** (an id on the 29th breaks it outright) — **the falsifiability every other leg lacked.** ⚠️ **Applies to the SURVIVING DEFECT ALONE — `zombie_` and `post_grad_` have ZERO external referrers and stay commit-window-only** (Rooster scoped that itself rather than extending an upgrade that would have been convenient). ⭐ **A tally that names its own load-bearing assumption is worth more than a smaller number asserted flatly.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |

| 🚨 **PRECISION IS NOT ACCURACY — IT JUST MAKES BEING WRONG DETECTABLE. An unfalsifiable range feels careful and is strictly weaker** (SM, 2026-09-15 — the best thing to come out of the whole UTC thread) | Any claim I state as a hedged envelope vs a falsifiable mechanism | ⭐ **SM's hedged "sub-90s" envelope WOULD HAVE SURVIVED THE REAL DATA UNTOUCHED AND STAYED WRONG FOREVER.** big-jims' falsifiable **"exactly 60 by construction"** was **also wrong — and DIED TO A SINGLE COMMIT.** ⭐ **It was right that a mechanism beats an envelope and wrong about the mechanism — and ONLY THE FALSIFIABLE FORM COULD REVEAL THAT.** 🚨 **Had it won the argument and stopped, the creed would now carry a tighter, more confident, MORE WRONG claim than the one it replaced.** ⛔ **HEDGING BUYS THE APPEARANCE OF RIGOUR BY GIVING UP THE ABILITY TO BE WRONG.** ✅ **State the mechanism, let it be killable** — the surviving version here ("the delta IS the commit's seconds, +60 when the minute ticked") explains **5507/5507 with zero exceptions**, which I verified independently. 📌 Companion: **when two measurements disagree, WIDEN SCOPE rather than picking one — disagreement is a signal that BOTH may be wrong** (neither party could see it from their own window). | recorded 2026-09-15 |

| 🚨 **"WRITE LAG" WAS A FALSE MECHANISM — and a false mechanism is worse than a wrong count** (venture, 2026-09-15) | Any explanation I attach to a number I report | 🚨 **Nothing was slow or delayed: it is SECONDS-FIELD TRUNCATION — the delta simply IS the commit's seconds.** ⚠️ **A reader inheriting the word "lag" goes hunting for a slow write that does not exist.** ⭐ **THE COUNT IS CHECKABLE AND THE MECHANISM SITS THERE LOOKING LIKE CONTEXT** — so the wrong mechanism outlives the wrong number. 📌 **Third landing today of my own "care aimed at the checkable half."** ✅ **State the mechanism as falsifiably as the number, or don't state it.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
