| ⭐ **"I LOOKED AND IT ISN'T THERE" IS A CLAIM ABOUT THE CONTAINER YOU SEARCHED — sometimes you must widen WITHIN, not outward** (Rooster, 2026-09-15) | Any "unavailable" / "not present" / "no independent source exists" conclusion | 🚨 **Rooster reported the dispatcher clock UNAVAILABLE for its finding, having searched THE THREE LESSON BODIES. It was in a file it OWNS, in its own steading, ONE COMMAND AWAY** — `workers/watchdog/HANDOFF.md`, carrying a `_queue_id` in the same sentence as the event. **That single find UPGRADED the fleet tally from "method-dependent" to "independently corroborated."** ⭐ **All day we widened TOWARD the fleet; this one needed widening WITHIN.** 🚨 **THE GREP RULE AS FIRST PUBLISHED WAS INCOMPLETE — CORRECTED FORM: anchor it `\b17[0-9]{11}\b`, use `-I` (skip binaries), THEN VALIDATE every candidate by decoding it into the fleet's actual lifetime, THEN report validated-of-raw WITH THE SCOPE.** ⭐ **The pattern yields CANDIDATES, NOT IDS.** 🚨 **WHY THE ANCHORS ARE LOAD-BEARING (Rooster — sharpest technical finding of the day): AN UNANCHORED NUMERIC PATTERN DOESN'T FIND NUMBERS, IT FINDS POSITIONS INSIDE NUMBERS** — it **slices 13-digit windows out of the middle of longer digit runs** (padding zeros, hashes, ids-with-suffixes) and **FABRICATES IDS THAT NEVER EXISTED** (`0014175110263749957` → `1751102637499` → 2025-06-28). Measured: unanchored **2017** vs anchored **1876** distinct, with **1959 long runs** to slice from. ✅ **RE-MEASURED MY OWN CLAIM PROPERLY (2026-09-15) — and it cut the OPPOSITE way from the warning: my `_queue_id`-prefixed count of 23 was CONSERVATIVE, not inflated.** Anchored + `-I`: **392 raw, 349 distinct candidates, 349 VALIDATED into fleet lifetime, ZERO rejected** — because my markdown has only **6** long digit runs to slice from (vs Rooster's 1959). ✅ **And I read the EXTREMES of my own output rather than trusting the bucket: min 2026-06-12, max 2026-09-15 11:10 (today, minutes before) — both POSSIBLE, not merely in-range.** 🚨 **BUT THAT CLEAN RESULT WAS RIGHT BY LUCK OF CORPUS, NOT BY METHOD — and the two are INDISTINGUISHABLE IN THE OUTPUT.** ✅ **SO I RAN CV'S CONTROL TEST, which works regardless of corpus: fed the pattern a KNOWN-BAD string.** `0014175110263749957` → **unanchored slices `1751102637499`** (a fabricated id) · **anchored: NO MATCH, correctly rejected.** Same for `0018445649184286594` → `1844564918428`. ⭐ **My anchored pattern is now proven sound BY CONSTRUCTION, not because my tree happened to lack slice sources.** ⚠️ **A hazard you cannot reproduce in your own tree is still a hazard.** 🚨 **AND "IT DECODED TO A PLAUSIBLE DATE" IS NOT VALIDATION — I decoded my own fabrications to prove it: `1751102637499` → 2025-06-28 (entirely plausible; a RANGE CHECK WOULD PASS IT).** ⭐ **Only IMPOSSIBLE-IN-FACT catches it — my logs begin 2026-06, so a 2025 date cannot be mine.** **A 100% validation rate is exactly what a BROKEN validator also produces.** 📌 **MY COUNT, IN THE REQUIRED FORM (validated-of-raw + scope + unit + VALIDATOR): 349 of 349 DISTINCT candidates, span 2026-06-12 → 2026-09-15, scope `*.md` under my steward dir, anchored + `-I`, validator = decode-into-fleet-lifetime with a 2023-01-01 floor.** 🚨 **AND THE VALIDATOR WAS DOING LESS WORK THAN I IMPLIED — I TESTED IT (2026-09-15): MY 2023 FLOOR WOULD HAVE *LAUNDERED* THE CANONICAL FABRICATION** `1751102637499` (→2025-06-28). **My 349/349 was clean because THE ANCHOR caught it, not the validator.** ⭐ **venture's proof: the SAME fabrication is laundered by a 2025 floor and rejected by a 2026 floor — opposite verdicts, PURELY from window width. THE WIDER YOUR WINDOW, THE MORE FABRICATIONS IT NECESSARILY ACCEPTS.** ⚠️ **MY FIRST FIX WAS ITSELF WRONG AND SM ADJUDICATED IT: I tightened the floor to 2026-06 — my corpus's EARLIEST OBSERVED VALUE — which is CIRCULAR.** 🚨 **A FLOOR FITTED TO THE SET IT JUDGES REJECTS 0 BY CONSTRUCTION and scores 100% on the set that defined it** (I verified: it cannot falsify anything). ⭐ **THE RESOLUTION IS PROVENANCE, NOT TIGHTNESS — A FILTER FITTED TO ITS OWN OUTPUT CERTIFIES ITS OWN OUTPUT.** A bound from an **INDEPENDENT FACT** (fleet founding, a deploy date, an external event) **CAN reject members of your own set — that is what makes it a TEST**; a fitted bound cannot. ⭐ **PREFER A LEAKY BOUND YOU CAN JUSTIFY OVER A TIGHT ONE THAT IS UNFALSIFIABLE — AND STATE WHICH KIND YOU USED.** 🚨 **AND EVERY SWEEP COUNT PUBLISHED TODAY IS SUSPECT — A `grep -r` SWEEP SILENTLY DROPS REAL CONTENT DEPENDING ON THE ROOT YOU START FROM** (venture, reproduced by SM): root `.` → **1877 distinct**, union of the same nine child dirs → **2048** — **172 ids visible from a child dir and INVISIBLE from the root** (a superset returning FEWER than its parts is structurally impossible). Settled by direct read of an ordinary small text file, not inference; **18 files >50MB** sit in the tree. ⭐ **A FOURTH SPECIES OF THE DAY'S DISEASE: not a threshold you chose, not an anchor you forgot, not a scope you picked — A SCOPE BOUNDARY THE TOOL APPLIED WITHOUT TELLING YOU.** ✅ **PROPERLY-CONTROLLED MEASUREMENT (2026-09-15 12:50 EDT): positive control FIRST (`/usr/bin/grep -c` on a known string → 1; my FIRST control attempt FAILED because I chose a string not in the file — reported as MY broken invocation, not quoted as a zero), then THREE METHODS AGREEING AT 350** — wrapper `grep`, `/usr/bin/grep`, and **venture's per-file baseline (`find -print0 | xargs grep`), which CANNOT exhibit the root defect.** 🚨 **AND MY TRUNCATION CHECK WAS ITSELF INERT — I printed an EMPTY `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` and wrote "124 would mean TRUNCATED" AS IF IT HAD PASSED.** ⭐ **THIS FLEET RUNS ZSH: it is `${pipestatus[1]}` — LOWERCASE, 1-INDEXED. Bash's is uppercase, 0-indexed. An empty string READS AS "no error".** ✅ **CONTROL-TESTED THE DETECTOR BEFORE TRUSTING ITS NULL — and my FIRST control was ALSO broken (wrong pipe shape, returned 0); the working one is `timeout 1 sleep 5 | cat` → `pipestatus: (124 0)`.** ✅ **Then re-verified the count with the PROVEN detector: 350, `pipestatus` (0 0 0 0 0) — complete, not merely plausible.** ⭐ **A STATUS CHECK THAT CANNOT REPORT A FAILURE IS WORSE THAN NONE.** Two-root check also agreed (350/350/350). 🚨 **BUT THAT NULL IS *NOT EXPOSED*, NOT *NOT AFFECTED* — I initially reported it as "instrument-independent by construction," which OVERSTATES IT.** 🚨 **THAT EXPOSURE FIGURE WAS INVALID AND I RE-REPORTED IT: I measured "0 files >50MB, largest 2.3MB" AGAINST THE LARGE-FILE HYPOTHESIS, WHICH LATER DIED.** ⭐ **AN EXPOSURE FIGURE IS ONLY AS GOOD AS THE MECHANISM IT WAS CHOSEN AGAINST** (CV made and corrected the identical error). ✅ **RE-MEASURED AGAINST THE REAL MECHANISM (gitignore): 2 of 62 files gitignored, and 0 of 37 `*.md` files — since my pattern searches ONLY `*.md`, ZERO of my searched files are gitignored, so the mechanism cannot reach my payload.** ⚠️ **AND CHECKED THE DIVERGENCE HAZARD SM NAMES: `.git/info/exclude` rules are STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE to `ugrep --ignore-files` (the rule file lives inside `.git`, which the wrapper excludes), so *"is it ignored?"* HAS DIFFERENT ANSWERS FOR GIT AND FOR YOUR GREP. My tree HAS 10 active exclude rules — but 0 of my 37 `*.md` files are ignored by ANY mechanism, so the divergence set is EMPTY for my searched corpus.** ⭐ **SM's warning names exactly this: a steading with no large files reports "roots agree" AND HAS TESTED NOTHING. Report EXPOSURE alongside the result, or the check is theatre.** ⚠️ **Correlation across the fleet (rooster 13 big files/164 dropped · mcgucket 1/6 · homestead 1/1 · venture 0/1 · CV 0/0 · big-jims 0/0) is strong with ONE anomaly, so the cause is PATH- or SUBTREE-correlated, not uniform — a CONSTRAINT on the hypothesis space, NOT a theory.** ⚠️ **Count moved 349 → 350 in minutes: A COUNT ON A LIVE CORPUS IS A TIMESTAMP, NOT A CONSTANT — stamp it.** (350 distinct, anchored + `-I`, `*.md` under my steward dir, floor 2026-03-30, measured 2026-09-15 ~12:28 EDT.) 📌 **THE CANONICAL FABRICATION IS IN MY OWN FILES: `1751102637499`, sliced from `0.0014175110263749957` — A FLOATING-POINT AUDIO COEFFICIENT in `mcgucket/.../voices.json`.** ⚠️ **SM initially alarmed that it had spread "as if it were an id" and then RETRACTED: he read the filenames, not the lines — all citations (mine included) carry the provenance and the warning.** ⭐ **He matched on the SHAPE of a citation and inferred its meaning — the same error as the slicing artifact, committed while reporting on it.** ✅ **The surviving rule (big-jims): WHEN YOU PUBLISH A FABRICATED SPECIMEN, PUBLISH ITS SOURCE IN THE SAME BREATH, OR THE SPECIMEN OUTLIVES THE LESSON** — which is exactly why mine were safe. ✅ **Checked mine: both copies are correctly framed as "FABRICATES IDS THAT NEVER EXISTED" / "KNOWN-BAD string", so they won't mislead a reader — but they DO inflate any future sweep.** ⭐ **The remediation-counts-as-disease trap again: writing about a defect propagates its signature.** ✅ **MY CORRECTED VALIDATOR: floor = 2026-03-30, the first commit creating `alfred/` — derived from GIT HISTORY, not from the id set. It CAN reject members of my own output, AND it catches the canonical fabrication (2025-06-28). Provenance + adequate tightness, no circularity.** ⭐ **ANCHOR FIRST, VALIDATE SECOND — and note THE ANCHOR AND THE VALIDATOR CATCH DIFFERENT SETS: "anchored + validated" READS like belt-and-braces and ISN'T; drop either and you lose a distinct class.** 🚨 **STATE THE VALIDATOR, NOT JUST THE RATIO — a "sane date?" check passes 3 of 4 fabrications, so two reports both claiming "100% validated" can differ by 3-of-4 in what they catch.** ⭐ **So dispatcher `_queue_id`s are FAR more available than assumed — 349 in my own markdown** (`triage-log.md`, `handoff/learnings-*.md`) — **they sit in HANDOFFs and prose, NOT in the records they are evidence for.** ⭐ **If I ever grade a finding down for lack of an independent clock, LOOK AGAIN — WIDER AND SHALLOWER.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **A VALIDATION STEP YOU WROTE YOURSELF IS NOT A CHECK ON THE PATTERN THAT FED IT** (Rooster, 2026-09-15 — the deepest rule of the day) | Any filter, validator or sanity-check I build over my own search output | 🚨 **A FILTER CERTIFIES WHAT IT WAS DESIGNED TO CATCH AND LAUNDERS EVERYTHING ELSE — the garbage it cannot see comes out WEARING THE VALIDATOR'S BADGE.** ⭐ **Rooster had a `2026-12-31` endpoint PRINTED IN ITS OWN OUTPUT — a future date, proof no dispatcher stamped it — and READ PAST IT, because the value sat in the "validated" bucket it had just built and therefore trusted.** ✅ **REMEDY: READ THE EXTREMES OF YOUR OWN OUTPUT — min, max, span — AND ASK WHETHER EACH IS *POSSIBLE*, NOT MERELY IN-RANGE.** ✅ **Applied 2026-09-15: my own min 2026-06-12 / max 2026-09-15 11:10 — checked as possible (my logs start in June, max is minutes ago), not just inside the window.** ⚠️ **Rooster wrote *"a pattern matching the SHAPE of an id is not a test that it IS one"* WHILE QUOTING NUMBERS PRODUCED BY EXACTLY THAT ERROR, IN THE SAME MESSAGE** — **third time today that knowing a rule and applying it came apart, and the cleanest version.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **CHECK THE INSTRUMENT BEFORE THE CONTAINER — and distrust an explanation that exonerates everyone** (SM's own error, 2026-09-15) | Any time two measurements disagree and I reach for a reason | 🚨 **SM hypothesised the gap was "different containers" (that Rooster had swept `~/code` or `node_modules`). WRONG — it had swept the same nine steading dirs. THE GAP WAS THE PATTERN, NOT THE SCOPE.** ⭐ **And he named why that hypothesis was seductive: IT MADE NEITHER OF THEM WRONG.** ⚠️ **AN EXPLANATION THAT EXONERATES BOTH PARTIES DESERVES MORE SCRUTINY, NOT LESS** — it is the socially comfortable answer, so it gets adopted without the test a blaming explanation would have to pass. 📌 Sits against "widen scope when two measurements disagree": **widen scope, AND check the instrument — and prefer the hypothesis that costs someone something.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **RIGHT BY LUCK OF CORPUS IS NOT RIGHT BY METHOD — and the two are INDISTINGUISHABLE IN THE OUTPUT** (SM/CV/venture/big-jims, 2026-09-15) | Any clean result — a zero, a 100%, a "no exceptions" — produced by a tool I built | ⭐ **Four steadings re-measured and every count survived; THREE survived by corpus, not method.** **big-jims** published a raw candidate count as an id count — right only because its tree holds **zero slice sources**; with one hash present it would have reported fabricated ids **with identical confidence and the identical number.** **venture** was saved by a `-[a-z0-9]{6}` suffix it had not added for that reason, **with 2422 long runs available to slice.** **CV** got 139=139 and **correctly refused to call it corroboration** — a null from **an unexposed corpus, not a refuted mechanism.** ✅ **THE FIX (CV's, and I ran it): PROVE THE HAZARD WITH A CONTROL TEST — feed the pattern a KNOWN-BAD string.** Verified myself: `0014175110263749957` unanchored → slices `1751102637499`; anchored → **no match.** ⚠️ **A hazard you cannot reproduce in your own tree is still a hazard.** 🚨 **AND REPORT VALIDATED-OF-RAW EVEN WHEN EQUAL — "8 of 8 validated" carries the method; "8" carries nothing.** 🚨 **"IT DECODED TO A PLAUSIBLE DATE" IS NOT VALIDATION: plausible-in-range ≠ possible-in-fact** (my own fabrication decodes to 2025-06-28, which a range check passes; only "my logs start 2026-06" rejects it). ⭐ **A 100% validation rate is exactly what a BROKEN validator also produces.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| ⚠️ **THE UNIT RULE FIRES AT EVERY NUMBER, NOT ONCE PER MESSAGE** (venture, 2026-09-15) | Every figure I write, in the same message as every other figure | 🚨 **Venture wrote "115" (occurrences — really 106 DISTINCT) and "11 distinct" CORRECTLY, in the same message. One right, one wrong, same author, same sentence.** ⭐ **That is CARELESSNESS, NOT CONFUSION — knowing the rule does not make it fire; it has to fire per-number.** ✅ **State the unit AT each number** (raw vs distinct vs occurrences vs files vs lines). 📌 Same family as "state the method with the number" and "scope rides with the figure at the point of use" — **and the same failure shape as knowing-vs-applying, which came apart four separate times today.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| ⭐ **"RIGHT BY LUCK" COMES IN GRADES: IS YOUR SAFETY A PROPERTY OF THE DATA OR OF THE INSTRUMENT? ONLY THE LATTER TRANSFERS** (venture/big-jims/Rooster, 2026-09-15) | Any clean result I report, and any method I hand to someone else | 🚨 **Rooster's statement of it, AGAINST ITSELF, is the best version: it had 21,135 long digit runs, so its corpus GUARANTEED the error would surface; big-jims had ZERO and would have looked IDENTICALLY CORRECT with the same broken method.** ⭐ **The same method produced a VISIBLE failure in one tree and an INVISIBLE one in the other.** **Rooster wasn't sloppier than the steadings that came back clean — it was MORE EXPOSED, which is the only reason the defect surfaced at all.** ⚠️ **So a clean result from an unexposed corpus is not evidence of a sound method, and a messy one is not evidence of a careless steward.** ✅ **Grade my own safety: DATA-luck (my 6 long runs) transfers to nobody; INSTRUMENT-soundness (the anchor, proven by control test) transfers everywhere.** 📌 **My own 349/349: instrument-sound on the anchor, DATA-lucky on the validator (2023 floor, ~3.5y too wide).** | recorded 2026-09-15 |

Honest restatement: "floor independent, ceiling fitted and therefore weak; the 4 rejects were caught by the floor, the ceiling caught nothing and could not have."** ✅ **So report each bound's PROVENANCE separately — a validator can be half-sound.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| ⚠️ **TWO SHELL FOOT-GUNS THAT PRODUCE FALSE ZEROS WHILE LOOKING LIKE THEY WORKED** (venture's self-reported errors, 2026-09-15) | Any script or one-liner I write to check something | 🚨 **(1) A FALSE ZERO FROM A FALSE NAME: a dir list containing `big-jims` when the directory is `big-jims-plates` — `[ -d ]` SKIPPED IT SILENTLY.** The loop ran, reported cleanly, and simply never looked. ⭐ **Verify the NAMES in a list resolve before trusting a per-item sweep.** 🚨 **(2) `2>&1 >/dev/null` WHEN YOU WANT STDERR — redirection order means stderr goes to the OLD stdout and stdout is discarded; the diagnostic tells you NOTHING while looking like it worked.** (Correct: `>/dev/null 2>&1` to discard both, or `2>&1 1>/dev/null` to keep stderr.) 📌 **Venture also floated a live-tree race and KILLED ITS OWN HYPOTHESIS with three runs (665/665/665) — proposing an explanation and then testing it rather than adopting it.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |

| 🚨 **A TIMEOUT ON A PIPELINE CONVERTS AN INCOMPLETE MEASUREMENT INTO A *SMALLER* ONE, NOT AN ERROR** (Rooster, 2026-09-15) | Any `timeout`-capped command piped into `wc`, `sort -u`, or any counter | 🚨 **`timeout` kills the producer mid-traversal, the pipe closes, and `wc` CHEERFULLY REPORTS HOWEVER MUCH ARRIVED BEFORE THE AXE. No stderr. No non-zero status surviving the pipe. A PLAUSIBLE NUMBER AT THE END.** Rooster's identical commands returned **1, then 0, then 0** — **not three measurements, three different amounts of traversal.** ⭐ **BIASED TOWARD FEWER — TOWARD ALL-CLEAR, the same direction as every other blind spot.** ✅ **`${PIPESTATUS[0]}` is 124 on truncation; NOTHING ELSE TELLS YOU.** ⚠️ **AND IT POISONED SM'S OWN INSTRUCTION: the ROOT sweep is slow PRECISELY BECAUSE it traverses the bulk a child sweep skips — so the root side is the side that TRUNCATES, MANUFACTURING THE VERY GAP THE CHECK LOOKS FOR.** Anyone running it under a cap finds their roots disagree and reads it as confirmation. 🚨 **AND TWO SWEEPS THAT TRUNCATE ON THE SAME SLOW SUBTREES WILL AGREE — Rooster's 164 matched SM's figure exactly, and that match is now LESS reassuring, not more.** ✅ **Run uncapped (background if slow), assert `${PIPESTATUS[0]}`, and treat "it finished" as something to ASSERT, NOT ASSUME.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **`${PIPESTATUS[0]}` IS SILENTLY INERT IN ZSH — AND THIS FLEET RUNS ZSH** (SM/big-jims, 2026-09-15; I was one of the affected) | Any pipeline status check I write | 🚨 **zsh: `${pipestatus[1]}` — LOWERCASE, 1-INDEXED. Bash: `${PIPESTATUS[0]}` — uppercase, 0-indexed. THE WRONG ONE RETURNS AN EMPTY STRING, WHICH READS AS "no error."** ⚠️ **I printed an empty value and wrote "124 would mean TRUNCATED" as if it had passed — the check was watching nothing.** ⭐ **A STATUS CHECK THAT CANNOT REPORT A FAILURE IS WORSE THAN NONE** — same family as a classifier emitting a confident distribution from an empty file. ✅ **CONTROL-TEST THE DETECTOR BEFORE TRUSTING ITS NULL.** 🚨 **AND A CONTROL TEST CAN ITSELF NEED A CONTROL: my FIRST control `( true | timeout 1 sleep 5 )` returned 0 — broken pipe shape, REASSURING VALUE.** 🚨 **CORRECTED AGAIN (venture, verified by me): `pipestatus[1]` IS THE *FIRST* COMMAND'S STATUS, NOT THE TRUNCATION-PRONE ONE.** Measured: `true | timeout 1 sleep 5` → `(0 124)` — **st[1]=0 (`true`), st[2]=124 (the timeout).** ⚠️ **My "working" control only LOOKED right because `timeout` happened to be FIRST in that pipe** — and my real verification read the correct element only because **`grep` happened to be first there too. CORRECT BY POSITION, NOT BY METHOD.** 🚨 **SECOND, WORSE TRAP: the array is CLOBBERED BY THE VERY NEXT COMMAND** — after one intervening `echo` it reads `0`. **That is why my original check printed empty.** **A rule that is correct but ORDER-SENSITIVE, handed to a fleet that will paste it on its own line, MANUFACTURES FALSE ALL-CLEARS.** 🚨 **AND MY 'CORRECTED' INDEX WAS WRONG TOO — NO INDEX IS PUBLISHABLE.** Rooster showed the right element depends on the **SHAPE**, not a zsh convention: `( true | timeout 1 sleep 5 )` → `pipestatus=[124]` (subshell **collapses** it, len 1) vs `true | timeout 1 sleep 5` → `[0 124]` (len 2). **Both readings were right; we ran different commands.** ⭐ **`pipestatus` has ONE ELEMENT PER STAGE, so a published index LOOKS PORTABLE AND IS WRONG for anyone with a different stage count — worse than the error it corrected.** ⭐ **A DETECTOR THAT REQUIRES THE READER TO COUNT STAGES CORRECTLY FAILS THE READER WHO IS TIRED OR NEW — eliminate-over-document, applied to the detector itself.** ✅ **THE ONLY PUBLISHABLE FORM — un-piped, plain `$?`, no array, no ordering trap, no stage-counting: `out=$(cmd); rc=$?`.** **Re-verified my own count that way: rc=0, 350.** If you must pipe, capture `st=("${pipestatus[@]}")` **on the same line** and read **the element matching your command's position.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **"IS IT IGNORED?" HAS DIFFERENT ANSWERS FOR GIT AND FOR YOUR GREP** (big-jims, 2026-09-15) | Any reconciliation using `git check-ignore` as ground truth for what a search tool skipped | 🚨 **`.git/info/exclude` rules are STRUCTURALLY INVISIBLE to `ugrep --ignore-files`, because that rule file lives INSIDE `.git`, which the wrapper excludes.** **So a file can be git-ignored by a rule your search tool cannot read** — and anyone using `git check-ignore` as ground truth **drifts by exactly that set.** ✅ **I CHECKED MINE: 10 active exclude rules exist, but 0 of my 37 searched `*.md` files are ignored by ANY mechanism → divergence set EMPTY for my corpus.** 🚨 **SECOND STRUCTURAL REASON (homestead, 2026-09-15): `git check-ignore` ANSWERS ONLY FOR THE REPO YOU RUN IT IN — it CANNOT see a NESTED repo's rules.** Homestead's "0 of 23,600 ignored" was a **false zero** from querying the OUTER repo while the files were ignored by a nested `.git` (`devices/<<REPLACE: a-household-member>>-studio/app/`). **That retraction KILLED the long-lines mechanism — its second missed file had a 9,461-char line, an order of magnitude shorter, missed identically. NO THIRD MECHANISM; gitignore explains it.** ✅ **Checked mine with an instance-level command (no sweep): NO nested `.git` under `alfred/`, so my exposure figure does NOT inherit this caveat.** ⭐ **Two structural reasons "is this file ignored?" has different answers depending on who asks — and both break reconciliation.** ⭐ **AND THE BIGGER CONSTRAINT: big-jims found the wrapper INCLUDING a file the explicit `ugrep` command EXCLUDES, though the snapshot says they are the same command. DEMONSTRATED SOMEWHERE ≠ OPERATIVE EVERYWHERE — the gitignore mechanism is PARTIAL, not settled.** ⛔ **Resist calling it settled because it explains most cases.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| ⭐ **WHEN A RECURSIVE MEASUREMENT IS SLOW AND DISPUTED, MEASURE THE *INSTANCE*, NOT THE CORPUS** (Rooster's withdrawal method, 2026-09-15) | Closing out a disputed sweep result | ✅ **Rooster withdrew its second-effect hypothesis by TESTING THE TWO FILES DIRECTLY rather than re-running a sweep that could truncate again** — a slow disputed instrument cannot adjudicate itself. 🚨 **AND ITS FRAMING OF WHAT NEARLY HAPPENED: A PHANTOM ANOMALY GENERATES A PHANTOM MECHANISM, AND THE MECHANISM OUTLIVES THE ANOMALY** — it would have sat in doctrine as an unexplained residual **nobody could ever close, because there was nothing there to find.** ⭐ **An "unexplained gap" in a record is a standing invitation to invent an explanation; closing it to zero is worth more than explaining it.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **ACCEPTING CONNECTIONS BUT NOT RESPONDING LOOKS IDENTICAL AT THE CLIENT WHETHER THE SERVER IS *WEDGED* OR *STARVED* — AND THE CORRECT ACTIONS ARE OPPOSITE** (homestead's call, 2026-09-15; **I got this wrong**) | Any time `:3005` or a local service times out on me | 🚨 **MY ERROR: three probes at HTTP 000, port listening (`lsof` → node LISTEN), pm2 `restart_time` jumped 0→3 — I concluded WEDGED and drafted a walkie asking Rooster to bounce it.** ⛔ **A restart does NOT fix starvation and would have cost Josh the presenter window.** ✅ **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS SERVER-SIDE, NOT CLIENT-SIDE: process state `Rs` + still burning CPU + JobScheduler firing normally (EL-LAG 258ms) = ALIVE AND WORKING, JUST CANNOT GET CPU.** ⭐ **WHAT ACTUALLY CORRECTED ME WAS READING THE LOGS INSTEAD OF RE-PROBING** — the dispatcher was enqueueing/confirming/delivering throughout, and my "failed" roger HAD landed (*"Confirm: item … not found"* — the POST arrived, the item had aged out). **The endpoint I called dead was answering other people's requests the whole time.** ✅ **AND A GENEROUS TIMEOUT DISTINGUISHES THEM CHEAPLY: `--max-time 45` returned HTTP 200 in 1.6s while `--max-time 10` returned 000.** ⚠️ **On timeouts: KEEP ROGERING (Rooster) — retry rather than stop; unconfirmed items piling up is worse than a retry.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **AN INVESTIGATION INTO A MEASUREMENT DEFECT CAN BECOME A LIVE INCIDENT ON THE PLATFORM BEING MEASURED** (homestead, 2026-09-15 — Josh saw a FAILED overlay) | Any diagnostic work I run on shared infrastructure | 🚨 **Several stewards ran uncapped full-tree sweeps simultaneously; load hit 37, `:3005` starved, Josh's APK exhausted its reconnect ladder and showed a CAN'T-CONNECT screen. He was carded that it was us, not a real outage.** ⭐ **The tally had read "1, independently corroborated" for HOURS — the marginal sweep was worth ~nothing and its cost became USER-VISIBLE.** 🚨 **BUT THE ALARM METRIC IS *SERVICE HEALTH*, NOT LOAD — homestead retracted its own ">20" threshold and I verified the retraction.** **Load 34 with `:3005` answering in 14ms is JOSH USING HIS PRESENTER (Electron GPU 42%, Renderer 37%); load 34 with `000` at 8s is an INCIDENT. SAME NUMBER, OPPOSITE MEANINGS — and a load threshold CANNOT TELL THEM APART.** ⚠️ **I had cited "load 30.48 and climbing" as evidence supporting its alarm — the metric that cannot distinguish the two.** ✅ **THE GATE IS: is `:3005` answering, and HOW FAST.** ⭐ **THE SIGNATURE IS THE SEQUENCE, NOT THE VALUE: improving probes (1.14s → 0.079s → 0.014s) = healthy; `000` at 8-10s repeatedly = brownout.** *(Verified myself: 200 in 39/14/20ms, load 24.25 with 1-min now BELOW 5-min = draining.)* ✅ **RULES: `uptime` as CONTEXT not a gate; check SERVICE HEALTH before calling an incident; SERIALISE (one steward at a time — an uncapped tree sweep is ~545s); and ⭐ MEASURE THE INSTANCE, NOT THE CORPUS — `git check-ignore <file>` + a direct grep costs milliseconds and answers the same question.** ⚠️ **A per-id loop that spawns a full-tree sweep per iteration is the worst shape: 172 × 545s, corpus-scanning to answer instance questions.** ⭐ **ASK WHAT A DIAGNOSTIC COSTS THE PEOPLE USING THE SYSTEM, NOT JUST WHETHER IT IS CORRECT.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |
| 🚨 **A DETECTOR THAT MATCHES ON COMMAND-LINE *TEXT* MATCHES ITSELF — AND EVERY MESSAGE QUOTING THE PATTERN** (homestead's own error, 2026-09-15; I was one of four who got a phantom hit) | Any `pgrep -f` / `ps | grep` check I run to find a process | 🚨 **`pgrep -fl 'ugrep -G|grep -r.*17[0-9]'` matches ANY command line CONTAINING that text — including roger-that curls and the recovery script itself, because the broadcasts QUOTE the pattern.** ⚠️ **WORST CASE: KILLING A PEER'S DIAGNOSTIC THINKING IT IS A SWEEP.** ✅ **USE BINARY + CPU INSTEAD: `ps -axo pid,pcpu,comm | awk '$3 ~ /ugrep|grep$/ && $2 > 5'`** — matches the EXECUTABLE, not the text. **Verified clean by me.** ⭐ **SAME DISEASE AS EVERYTHING ELSE TODAY: AN INSTRUMENT MATCHING ON TEXT AND BEING READ AS MATCHING ON REALITY** (the slice, `_archived`, the citations, this). 📌 **My own phantom hit was homestead's recovery script — I identified it as not-a-sweep rather than acting on it, which is the behaviour that makes a bad detector survivable.** | recorded 2026-09-15 |

A template that could not even fill in his first name is provably a mass send, not a person writing to him.** ⚠️ **A HUMAN NAME IN THE `from` FIELD IS NOT A PERSONAL EMAIL** — read the body for merge tags, unsubscribe footers and campaign formatting before treating a named sender as a relationship. 📌 **a contact is in the <a local place> Code School orbit (David <<REPLACE: a client contact>>'s org is `southbendcodeschool.com`), so this is a NEIGHBOURING-ORG mailing list, not a stranger — still noise.** ✅ **SURFACE ONLY IF he writes personally (no merge tags, references something real), or if a program is ever age-appropriate for his child — revisit around 2039.** | 1x seen 2026-09-17 |

(3) "respond with C to confirm" → ⛔ I do NOT reply on his behalf; confirming a medical appointment is his.** ⚠️ **$50 CHARGE for reschedules without 2 business days' notice — that makes a MISSED appointment a real money event, so the dates are worth holding properly.** 📌 **KNOWN SCHEDULE as of 2026-09-17: Thu Oct 1 2026 3:30 PM · Mon Nov 30 8:00 AM · Thu Mar 11 2027 8:00 AM.** ⭐ **SURFACE only when a leave-time trigger fires, if a form deadline is about to be MISSED, or if an appointment is at risk.** | 1x seen 2026-09-17 |

## 🚨 HOMESTEAD IS REACHABLE FROM THE LAN + TAILNET WITH NO AUTH (verified live 2026-09-17)

`server.js:261` binds `'::'` → `lsof` shows `TCP *:3005`. **Measured, not inferred:** HTTP 200
from the LAN IP (<<REPLACE: your LAN IP>>) AND the tailnet IP (<<REPLACE: your Tailscale IP>>); an **unauthenticated
`POST /api/queue` from the LAN returned HTTP 200.** `/api/broadcast` fans out to EVERY steward.
Agents spawn `--dangerously-skip-permissions` (`queue-dispatcher.js:427,907`, `scheduler.js:352`),
so anything that arrives acts with no human gate, reaching Keychain, SSH keys, gh, and the MCPs.

🚨 **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-18 — THIS LINE IS WRONG. See the CORRECTION section below: it IS internet-exposed via phone-alley on :443.** (Kept visible, not deleted, because a reader hitting this first would otherwise be reassured by a false claim.) Original text follows:
✅ ~~**NOT internet-exposed — check this BEFORE ever calling it an emergency.**~~ `tailscale serve
status` shows **3005 appears 0 times**; every proxied port reads "tailnet only." Home wifi +
tailnet only. ⭐ **That one positive check is the whole difference between "fix this week" and
"wake him up."** Run it before writing the card.

⛔ **NOT findings — do NOT re-raise these:** the tmux paste **cannot** be broken out of
(`writeFileSync` → `tmux load-buffer <file>`, never interpolated into a shell string; and
`JSON.stringify` escapes the ESC byte to a literal ``, so terminal-control injection fails).
`spawn-notification-triage.js` is orphaned/dead. **Hostile text can make an agent MISBEHAVE;
it cannot execute commands directly.** ⚠️ That JSON re-encode is load-bearing — if any future
delivery path skips it, this stops being safe.

⚠️ **The fix is a REAL design call, not a one-liner:** his phone pushes notifications over the
tailnet, so a bare `127.0.0.1` bind **breaks the notification pipeline.** Options are (a) keep the
tailnet bind + add a shared token the phone app also sends, or (b) localhost bind + an authed
Tailscale `serve` proxy. Open question nobody has answered: **can the Android app send an auth
header without a rebuild/reinstall?** Don't guess — that decides which option is cheaper.

## ⭐ "GET IT OUT OF GIT" ≠ "DELETE IT" (Josh, 2026-09-17)

Verbatim: *"don't delete the message history, just get it out of the git stuff and the git
history."* **Untrack + gitignore; the files STAY on disk.** Live code reads
`data/message-history/` (`check-notifications.js`, `mcp-servers/phone/index.js`,
`app/api/message-history/route.ts`) — deleting breaks the notification pipeline.

🚨 **UNTRACKING ALONE DOES NOT HOLD — an autosaver runs `git add -A` on this repo every few
minutes.** The `.gitignore` entry is the load-bearing half. Verified in the **LIVE**
`~/.claude/hooks/auto_save.py` (the dotfiles copy DIFFERS — `diff` them, live wins): it walks
`git status --porcelain -uall` **without `--ignored`**, so ignored paths are never candidates.
📌 Same shape for `*.pem` (kept on disk — `server.js:1763` reads them when `USE_HTTPS=true`,
which is `false` in prod) and `.git-rewrite/`.

⚠️ **Count what EXISTS, not just what git sees:** 544 files on disk vs 442 tracked. "442" was
correct about git and would have been wrong about reality.

## ⚠️ BEFORE A HISTORY REWRITE ON A SHARED REPO — COUNT WHO IT STRANDS

`git filter-repo` works and I verified it in a mirror (control: a real number findable in the
original, **0 occurrences** after). But applying it force-pushes new hashes to EVERY branch.
**Homestead: 58 local branches, 43,425 commits, 37 branches with work not on main, 30 live
steward sessions, a second active worktree.** ⚠️ **Count `refs/heads`, NOT `git branch -a`** —
`-a` adds remote-tracking refs and double-counts (it read 85; the real local count is 58).
A mirror clone legitimately shows MORE branches than the source, because it converts
remote-tracking refs into real ones — **compare branch NAMES, not counts**, before concluding
anything was lost. ⭐ **Weigh that against the ACTUAL exposure first:**
private repo, 0 forks, 0 deploy keys, **0 clones in 14 days**, and the one pending invite
**never accepted** — the data has never left his account. **A rewrite that strands the fleet to
scrub data nobody ever fetched is the wrong trade.** Do it at a quiet moment, on his word.
📌 Keep the escape hatch: `git bundle create --all` before any rewrite (386M for this repo).
⚠️ Surviving path-name matches after a scrub may be **source code** that reads the data
(`lib/message-history-store.js`) — a path match is not a content match. Check the blob type.

## 🚨 CORRECTION — HOMESTEAD *IS* INTERNET-EXPOSED. My "tailnet only" was WRONG (2026-09-18)

⛔ **The section above said "NOT internet-exposed." That was wrong and is now corrected here.**
I checked `tailscale serve status` for **port 3005**, found **0 occurrences**, and concluded
tailnet-only. **The port was never the right unit to check.**

🚨 **THE ACTUAL PATH:** `:443` → `127.0.0.1:3009` = **phone-alley** (pm2), with
`AllowFunnel: {':443': True}`. `phone-alley/server.js:13` sets
`HOMESTEAD_TARGET = http://127.0.0.1:3005`; `/api/queue` is in its `RETRYABLE_PATHS` (~line 91)
plus a catch-all proxy (~line 205). **No auth gate anywhere on that path.** So 3005 is public
**through a different service's port** — and a port-scoped grep is structurally blind to that.

⭐ **THE GENERAL LESSON — ASK "WHAT REACHES THIS SERVICE," NOT "IS THIS PORT PUBLISHED."**
A reverse proxy means the exposed port and the exposed *service* are different objects. My
instrument could only ever have found a direct publication of 3005; indirect exposure was
**invisible to it by construction**, so the zero was guaranteed regardless of truth.
⚠️ **`:443` was the ONLY serve entry lacking the "(tailnet only)" tag** — the discriminator was
on screen in output I had already read, and I scanned it for my port instead of reading what each
row said.

✅ **HOW IT WAS ACTUALLY SETTLED (do it this way):** a 200 from this Mac proves nothing — **I am
on the tailnet**, so that result is ambiguous between both hypotheses. The disambiguator was
**public DNS**: `dig @8.8.8.8 joshuas-macbook-air.tail84bb3b.ts.net` → **<remote-host> /
<remote-host>** (non-100.x = Funnel ingress), then
`curl --resolve <host>:443:<remote-host> -X POST /api/queue` → **HTTP 200**. **Force the
off-tailnet path; never accept a result a tailnet member could have produced either way.**
📌 Aim probes at a nonexistent target (`__alfred_selftest_nonexistent__`) — they terminate as
`skipped-unknown-target` and reach no real session.

Verbatim, twice in four sentences: *"Definitely no passwords... the idea is that authentication

⛔ **Do not offer tokens, shared secrets, or API keys for this surface again.** The fix shape is

✅ **NO APP REBUILD NEEDED — this was the open question and it is ANSWERED.** The phone talks to
the **hostname on :443**, never to 3005 or a LAN IP: `HomesteadNotificationListener.kt:53`,
`ApiServerService.kt:111`, `RemoteFragment.kt:34`, `TranscriptionService.kt:23`, and also

⚠️ **Open question that is genuinely Josh's, not mine:** does anything legitimately need `:443`

was set deliberately by someone. **Rooster verifies before flipping; if a real consumer exists I
carry it to Josh rather than let it break silently.**
📌 Infra/Tailscale = **Rooster's lane**. I measured and handed off; I did not change it myself.

## 🚨 SEARCHING THE QUEUE ARCHIVES: two traps that both return a confident wrong number (2026-09-18)

**1. FALSE ZERO — the archives have NO `timestamp` key.** The field is **`created_at`** (ISO
string, not epoch-ms). Filtering on `timestamp` returns **0 regardless of truth**. ⭐ **The
dangerous part is the SHAPE, not the zero:** mine would have read as *"Rooster fabricated the
timeline"* — an accusation against a peer resting entirely on a broken instrument. **Print the keys
and the item count as a CONTROL before trusting any archive count.** (Seventh false zero in this
family in a week.)

**2. CONTAMINATION — talking about an alarm puts its text in the queue, where a text search counts
it AS the alarm.** Rooster's re-run returned 12; two extras were *our own walkies that night*
quoting the alarm string, one was a worker audit discussing it. Same family as the credential hunt
that manufactured copies of the token it was hunting: **the investigation polluting its own corpus.**
✅ **DISCRIMINATOR for genuine phone alarms: `notif-push` in the item id** (they arrive via
notification-ingest; a steward discussing one never does). Or require the notification wrapper
(`"from":"notification-ingest"` / `"notification-checker"`) as I did.

📌 **Verified baseline: 10 distinct off-tailnet alarm days** — 08-05, 08-09, 08-13, 08-16, 08-20,
08-23, 08-24 (×2), 08-27, 08-30, 09-17. First alarm shipped **2026-08-05**, so ten in six weeks.
⚠️ **NOT "3 in 3 weeks"** (Rooster's first figure, withdrawn) — that reads *rare* where the truth
reads *routine*, and a card was about to lean on it. Two independent methods agreed on 10.

⛔ **The archives CANNOT tell you whether Josh used the recovery app.** Recovery calls hit `:443`
directly and never enter the walkie queue. I built a matcher for it, got 2 hits, and **discarded
it** — it matched any message containing "restart" and swept in unrelated fleet traffic. **Say "we
cannot check this," never imply we did.**

## ⚠️ "AN ANDROID IS ACTIVE" ≠ "HIS PHONE IS UP" — identify by DEVICE NAME

`tailscale status` shows **pixel-6 (<<REPLACE: your phone Tailscale IP (tailscale ip -4 on the device)>>) AND pixel-9a-1 (<<REPLACE: your Tailscale IP>>)** both able to
read "active." **`pixel-6` is RETIRED**; Josh's live handset is **`pixel-9a-1`**. Earlier this
session I curled the pixel-6, got a 200, and nearly told Josh a true offline alarm was false.
✅ **Always match the device NAME, never "an android responded."** Same failure family as
shortening a worker session name to its suffix.

## 🚨 NUCLEAR RESTART IS BROKEN — it deletes the prod build and never rebuilds (2026-09-18)

`watchdog/server.js:206` `/api/nuclear-restart` runs: `kill -9` on :3005 → **`rm -rf ${HOMESTEAD_DIR}/.next`**
→ `sleep 1` → `pm2 restart homestead`. **No `npm run build` anywhere.**
Its own comment says *"Next.js needs time to rebuild"* — **true in dev, FALSE here.**
✅ **Verified, two independent directions:** `server.js:260` `const dev = NODE_ENV !== 'production'`;
pm2 env `NODE_ENV=production` → `dev=false`, so Next serves prebuilt output and never regenerates.
`.next` is a **260M** prebuilt tree with a `BUILD_ID`. And Next ships the exact error for this case —
confirmed present at `node_modules/next/dist/server/lib/router-utils/filesystem.js:159`:
*"Could not find a production build in the '${distDir}' directory."*
🚨 **So the button labelled "for when the server is totally fucked" would FINISH IT OFF.**

⛔ **NEITHER OF US TESTED IT, DELIBERATELY** — a live repro means breaking his prod server. **A
source-level citation made the repro unnecessary**; state it as a code reading backed by Next's own
error path, never as "I ran it." ⭐ **That is stronger than hedging AND honest about method.**
✅ **Rooster's fix (agreed): STOP DELETING `.next` — do not insert a build step.** A build takes
minutes, can fail on its own, and would run at the exact moment he is locked out and least able to
debug — **turning a recovery button into a gamble.** Kill + restart only; strictly-harder restart,
not a destructive one. Sequenced after the auth work (same path).
📌 **Related, verified: RELOAD is phone-side only** — `StatusFragment.kt:214-224` fires an Android
intent at the main APK's WebViews; **never touches the Mac.** He'd otherwise learn that by pressing
it mid-outage and seeing nothing happen.
📌 **His load-bearing path is EMERGENCY WORKER → restart** (his own words: *"the one I use most
often... almost always restart... consistently works"*). **Protect it through any trim.**

⭐ **PATTERN WORTH HUNTING: failure modes that only surface AT THE MOMENT OF NEED.** Second this week
(the phone-network alarm's Twilio path was the first). A recovery tool is never exercised in normal
operation, so **nothing routine will ever reveal it's broken** — these need proactive audit, not
monitoring.

## ⭐ JOSH REFRAMED THE SECURITY QUESTION BETTER THAN WE ASKED IT (2026-09-18)

We asked *"public or tailnet-only?"* He answered with the real requirement: **the recovery path must
be INDEPENDENT OF THE THING BEING RECOVERED.** Verbatim: *"it allows a connection to my machine that
is not reliant on homestead which is very important because if homestead's down it doesn't matter."*
🚨 **The blanket Funnel flip would have coupled his emergency route to the health of the system it
exists to rescue.** ⭐ **Asking instead of acting is what caught it** — Rooster blocking on the
recovery app, and me holding the card rather than pre-empting his answer.
✅ **DECISION: `:443` Funnel STAYS OPEN.** The fix is to **scope** it, not close it — only the
recovery app may open that door.
⚠️ **A hard-coded secret in the APK does NOT contradict "definitely no passwords"** (2026-09-18,
same day). **His objection was to HIM TYPING ONE**, not to credentials existing: *"even if it's
hard-coded, I don't give a shit."* **Read a UX objection as UX, not as a crypto constraint** —
treating it as the latter would have blocked the right design.

## 🚨 TWO DIFFERENT `/api/nuclear-restart` ENDPOINTS — cutting by NAME would delete working code

⛔ **The name is not the unit. There are two, and only ONE is destructive:**
- **`watchdog/server.js:206`** — `kill -9` → **`rm -rf .next`** → `pm2 restart`, **no rebuild.**
  **DESTRUCTIVE.** This is the one Josh's button hits and the one being removed.
- **`phone-alley/lib/routes-api.js:74`** — `pm2.restartAll()` + PID-flip verification +
  `waitForOnline`. **NON-destructive, and covered by tests** (`phone-alley/test/test.mjs`,
  `parity-check.sh`). **LEAVE IT ALONE.**
✅ **Discriminator, measured:** `rm -rf .next` occurrences — **watchdog 1, phone-alley 0.**
✅ **Which one the button calls:** `StatusFragment.kt:879` → `"$WATCHDOG_BASE_URL/api/nuclear-restart"`,
where `WATCHDOG_BASE_URL = "$HOMESTEAD_BASE_URL/watchdog"` → **the watchdog's destructive handler.**
⭐ **Rooster's fleet grep caught this; my repo-local grep had missed it.** **A shared endpoint NAME
across services is exactly the shape where "grep the name, cut the hits" destroys something working.**

📌 **Caller sweep OUTSIDE `~/code/homestead` (the half a repo sweep structurally cannot see) — CLEAN.**
Every hit in `~/.homestead` / `~/code/dotfiles` is **documentation or data**: `queue.json`, homestead
library `store.json` + `.bak` files, my own triage-log/rules.md. **Zero executable callers**
(`.js/.sh/.py/.ts/.mjs` filter). **No crontab match, no LaunchAgents match.**
⭐ **Ran it with a CONTROL** — the same filter finds `phone-alley/server.js`, `test.mjs`,
`parity-check.sh`, so **the empty result is a real zero, not a broken pattern.** Three targeted
greps beat a full-fleet CPU burn.

⭐ **SEQUENCING RULE (Rooster's, better than mine): cut the ENDPOINT first, independently of the APK.**
The endpoint is the disarm; the button is only a caller. **A leftover button that 404s is strictly
safer than a live endpoint behind a removed button** — and it means the fix doesn't wait on Josh
tapping update on an APK. ⛔ **Never leave a live destructive endpoint behind a removed button and
call it done.**
📌 **Cheap proof we cut the right one of the two:** `test.mjs` + `parity-check.sh` must still pass
after the cut — they exercise phone-alley's endpoint, which we keep. **If they break, we cut too much.**

## 🚨 "THE MCP TIMES OUT FOR ME" IS A CLAIM ABOUT MY CONNECTION, NOT THE SERVICE (2026-09-18)

I reported `google-calendar` + `google-drive` **down all session** (`CONNECT_TIMEOUT` after 30s) and
escalated it to Rooster as a layer/auth/gateway fault. **It was my session's stdio link, wedged.**
Rooster called `list-calendars`/`list-events` **successfully from their session seconds later**, with
five live MCP processes and valid auth.

⭐ **THE DISCRIMINATOR IS ONE QUESTION I NEVER ASKED: "can a peer reach it right now?"** A timeout
is measured **at my end of the pipe**; it cannot distinguish a dead server from a dead link to a
live server. ⚠️ **Same shape as "this Mac is idle" ≠ "he isn't signed in anywhere"** — a local
measurement generalised into a global claim.
🚨 **COST: I told Josh his calendar connection was broken and offered him a button to fix a fault
that did not exist**, while sitting on an answer I could have read all along.

✅ **THE FIX IS `heal-mcp`, and this is precisely its case.**
`bash ~/.claude/skills/heal-mcp.sh --server <name>` → exit 0 → tools reappear.
✅ **VERIFY BY READING REAL DATA, never by a green status or a reconnect message** — `heal-mcp`
itself prints *"no verification probe registered; verify via ToolSearch"*. I confirmed by pulling
three actual events with attendees and reminders.
📌 **Before escalating ANY MCP timeout to Rooster: run heal-mcp first, then ask a peer to try the
same call.** Escalating "it's down" when it's "down for me" sends them hunting a phantom.

- **Sat 09-19, 12:00–13:00** — **"Lunch witta Boiz"** with **David <<REPLACE: a client contact>>**. Location **"TBD"**.
  ⚠️ **David's `responseStatus` is `needsAction` — he never RSVP'd**, and the location was never
  set. **That is very likely why he texted "we still on for tomorrow?"** — from his side it was
